 succeeded in 0ms:
   355	### 3.1 `format`
   356	
   357	Tagged by `type`:
   358	
   359	**`single`** — one label of possibly dynamic size:
   360	
   361	```yaml
   362	format:
   363	  type: single
   364	  width: { min: 10.0, max: "{target_width}" }   # Dynamic Dimension referencing parameter
   365	  height: 12.0                                  # Dimension (fixed or "{param}")
   366	  media_width: 24.0                             # optional; nominal tape width in template unit
   367	```
   368	
   369	| Field | Type | Required | Notes |
   370	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
   371	| `width` | `Dimension` | Yes | Label width. Fixed number, parameter reference `"{param}"`, or dynamic object `{ min, max }` for auto-length. |
   372	| `height` | `Dimension` | Yes | Label height. Fixed number or parameter reference `"{param}"`. |
   373	| `media_width` | number > 0 | No | Nominal tape/media width in the template `unit`. Declares the physical media this template targets. Used exclusively by the print preflight gate (see the Printing section). Has no effect on rendering geometry. The bundled Brother tape templates set this field (12, 18, or 24 mm). |
   374	
   375	A `Dimension` is either a fixed value (a literal number or parameter reference `"{param}"`), or a dynamic
   376	range object `{ min, max }` (where `min` and `max` are numbers or parameter references). A `single`
   377	template with a dynamic `format.width` is **auto-length**: the label width is determined at render time
   378	by measuring the content, clamping to `[min, max]`, and choosing the largest font that fits the budget
   379	(then ellipsis if still over). `auto` item width on a dynamic-width label resolves to the content width
   380	(`label_width - at.x`). Both `min` and `max` must be present; a missing bound is `422 TemplateInvalid`.
   381	At load time, parameter defaults are instantiated to validate default geometry bounds. Sheet templates
   382	and fixed-width single templates are unaffected. See [ADR-0026](adr/0026-auto-length-dynamic-width.md),
   383	[ADR-0030](adr/0030-multiline-auto-length-tape.md), and [ADR-0055](adr/0055-parameterized-templates.md).
   384	
   385	**Multiline text on dynamic-width singles.** `multiline: true` is supported on auto-length `single`
   386	templates (ADR-0030). Wrapping uses the item's auto-width budget (`width.max - at.x`, minus container
   387	padding). The renderer shrinks from `font_size.max` toward `font_size.min` (0.5 pt steps) until the
   388	text fits; line count is emergent (`floor((H − overflow + leading) / (cap_height + leading))` at the
   389	chosen font size, where `H` is the text item's box height and `overflow` is the ink reservation for
   390	`top`/`bottom` alignment described below — zero for `center`). If the
   391	content still overflows at `font_size.min`, the fitting lines are kept and the last is ellipsized.
   392	The tape label extent is `at.x + longest_wrapped_line_width`, clamped to `[width.min, width.max]`.
   393	Wrapped lines are precomputed in the measurement pass and emitted verbatim in the render pass
   394	(each line's spaces replaced with non-breaking spaces so Typst cannot re-break them).
   395	
   396	**`alignment.vertical` on auto-length items.** The schema default is `top`. Auto-length items that
   397	omit `alignment.vertical` use `top`. To keep text centered, set `vertical: center` explicitly. The
   398	bundled tape templates already set `vertical: center` and are unaffected.
   399	
   400	**What `alignment.vertical` aligns.** It positions a **fixed metric box**, so the baseline lands in
   401	the same place regardless of which glyphs a string contains: `test`, `testj` and `es` from one
   402	template all sit on one line. The box is Typst's default, cap-height to baseline — the same box CSS
   403	`text-box-trim` and Figma's vertical trim use, and the convention Pango, Pillow, TeX (`\strut`) and
   404	Flutter (`StrutStyle`) all follow (ADR-0045, #133).
   405	
   406	Consequences worth knowing when authoring a template:
   407	
   408	- **`top` and `bottom` inset the block so its ink stays inside the slot.** The line box runs
   409	  cap-height to baseline, so `g j p q y` fall below it and accented capitals (`É`) rise above it —
   410	  outside the clipped slot. A `top`- or `bottom`-aligned block is therefore padded at its aligned edge
   411	  by the font's overflow there (`ascender − cap_height`, `|descender|`; both 0.2412em in Inter), and
   412	  the auto-shrink fitter reserves *both* overflows for such items so the edge the text is not aligned
   413	  to cannot clip either. A height-bound `top`/`bottom` item consequently fits at a smaller size than
   414	  the cap-height box alone would allow (ADR-0050).
   415	- **Two limits worth knowing.** `center` is not inset: it splits the slack already, and reserving both
   416	  sides would cost a full em and shrink every centered template. Centered text can therefore still
   417	  clip in a slot shorter than `1.21 × font_size`. And the reservation covers the font's
   418	  ascender/descender band, not glyphs outside it — Inter has 211 that ink above its ascender and 197
   419	  below its descender (`Ǻ`, circled letters), which can still clip at any alignment.
   420	- **Lowercase-only text sits lower in its slot than all-caps.** The box always reserves cap-height
   421	  space above the baseline whether or not the string uses it. This is inherent to baseline alignment,
   422	  not a bug — it is what every other renderer produces.
   423	- Blank first/last lines are dropped before rendering, so a leading or trailing newline does not push
   424	  the visible text off centre; interior blank lines are kept as spacing.
   425	
   426	**`sheet`** — a grid of identical label slots on a fixed page:
   427	
   428	```yaml
   429	format:
   430	  type: sheet
   431	  paper_width: 8.5
   432	  paper_height: 11.0
   433	  label_width: 4.0
   434	  label_height: 2.0
   435	  positions:           # bottom-left corner of each slot, page origin bottom-left
   436	    - [0.18, 8.5]
   437	    - [4.32, 8.5]
   438	```
   439	
   440	## 4. Layout
   441	
   442	`layout` is an ordered list of layout items, rendered back-to-front (later items draw on top). Items
   443	are tagged by `type`. All items share a **placement** (flattened into the item):
   444	
   445	| Field | Type | Default | Meaning |
   446	| --- | --- | --- | --- |
   447	| `at` | `[x, y]` | `[0, 0]` | Lower-left anchor, in template units; may be edge-relative (see §6). |
   448	| `size` | `[w, h]` | — | Each entry is a number, `auto`, or parameter reference `"{param}"`. Mutually exclusive with `to`; exactly one is required (a `container` defaults to `[auto, auto]`). |
   449	| `to` | `[x, y]` | — | Opposite (top-right) corner; `size = to - at` after both corners resolve. Mutually exclusive with `size`. |
   450	| `max_w` / `max_h` | number | — | Upper bound that **caps** the resolution of `auto` on its axis, in validation, measurement, and rendering, on every format. An error alongside `to`, which has no `auto` axis. |
   451	| `rotate` | number (deg) | — | **Container only.** Orthogonal CCW rotation of the container's inner content; see §4.2. An error on any non-container item. |
   452	| `when` | map | — | Optional conditional rendering gate `{ param: value }`. Item renders only if all entries match resolved parameters. Supported on all item types. See §5. |
   453	
   454	`auto` size resolves to `min(max_{w,h}, fallback)` when both are present, to whichever of the two is
   455	present when only one is, and is an error when neither is. The fallback, for `text` and `container`, is
   456	the parent frame's dimension **remaining from `at`** on that axis — `frame - resolved_at`, using the
   457	resolved (not raw) anchor, so an edge-relative `at` (§6) is measured inward from the far edge before the
   458	subtraction — not the whole frame, which is space the item's own position has already claimed. This
   459	applies at both the validation and render layers, on every format. On a dynamic-width `single` template
   460	(§3.1), `auto` width instead resolves to the content width (`label_width - at.x`) derived from the
   461	pre-render measurement pass for `text`, but still to the frame remainder for `container` — the resolution
   462	basis differs by item type even though both are "the fallback" in prose. `qr` and `image` have no
   463	fallback at all: neither has a natural content footprint to shrink to, so their `auto` requires an
   464	explicit `max_w` and resolves to exactly that width on every format; without `max_w` it is an error, not
   465	a fill (ADR-0053). `auto` handling is not fully uniform across item types (ADR-0054): the `qr`/`image`
   466	fallback exclusion, the content-width-vs-frame-remainder split above, and the zero-remainder split below
   467	are three distinct asymmetries, not one. `max_w` / `max_h` bind **only** `auto`: a numeric `size`
   468	component is never clamped by them, at any layer. A non-`auto` numeric size must be > 0. (`line` does not
   469	use `size`; see §4.1.)
   470	
   471	A fallback (or a `max_*`-capped resolution) of exactly `0` is not automatically an authoring error. On a
   472	dynamic-width `single` template, a `container`'s own `auto` **width** axis resolving to `0` renders an
   473	empty (zero-width) box rather than erroring — a legitimate outcome of measurement, not a mistake
   474	(ADR-0053, ADR-0054 § Correcting ADR-0053). Every other `auto` axis that resolves through the shared
   475	fallback helper — `text` on either axis, a `container`'s **height** axis on any format, and a
   476	`container`'s **width** axis on any non-dynamic-width format — treats a
   477	`<= 0` result as an error: `size_auto_no_room` when no `max_*` is the binding value, `max_size_invalid`
   478	when a `max_*` is (§10.1).
   479	
   480	On a dynamic-width label, `max_w` therefore *caps a content-sized item* rather than being inert: `auto`
   481	there already means "shrink to content" (§3.1), and the bound further limits how much content-driven
   482	width an item may claim, capping both what the measurement pass reserves and what rendering draws. What
   483	changed under ADR-0053 is that the pre-render measurement pass now reserves the capped width for
   484	`qr`/`image` instead of the whole remaining budget. (ADR-0053)
   485	
   486	### 4.1 Item types
   487	
   488	- **`text`** — `value` (interpolated template, see §8), plus
   489	  placement, `font_size`, `font_weight` (optional literal or `"{param}"` reference), `multiline` (default `false`),
   490	  `alignment` (`horizontal`: left/center/right, `vertical`: top/center/bottom; default `top`), and optional `when` gate.
   491	  `font_weight` is a multiple of 100 between 100 and 900 (or an integer parameter resolving to one); omitting it leaves the font's default
   492	  (400). The bundled Inter is a variable font, so every step in that range is a real weight rather
   493	  than a synthetic one.
   494	  `font_size` is either a fixed number or a range `{ min, max }`. A range auto-shrinks the text to fit
   495	  the box (0.5pt steps) and truncates with an ellipsis if it still overflows.
   496	  `multiline: true` also changes the print form: that item's data fields render as a textarea rather
   497	  than a single-line input, so line breaks can be typed directly (#148). A field also used by a
   498	  single-line item *anywhere* in the template is flagged there, because that item renders only its first line.
   499	  Single-line text collapses spaces to non-breaking and renders only the first line. On dynamic-width
   500	  `single` templates, `multiline: true` is also supported; see §3.1 for the wrap and sizing rules.
   501	- **`qr`** — `value` (interpolated template, see §8), plus
   502	  placement, optional `when` gate, optional `params`:
   503	  `error_correction` (`L`/`M`/`Q`/`H`, default `M`), `module_size`, `quiet_zone`.
   504	  Rendered as an SVG via the `qrcode` crate, embedded as a Typst image.
   505	- **`image`** — exactly one of `src` (a path to a bundled asset, resolved under the assets root with a
   506	  traversal guard) or `name` (a data key whose value is a base64 data URI, `data:<mime>;base64,...`),
   507	  plus placement, optional `when` gate, and optional `fit` (`contain` default, `cover`, `stretch`). Formats: PNG, JPEG, SVG.
   508	  Bytes are decoded server-side and injected into Typst as a virtual file; there is no server-side URL
   509	  fetching (see ADR-0009). The assets root is `{config}/assets/` (`LABELER_CONFIG_DIR`). Missing data
   510	  key → `MissingField`; bad base64 / unsupported format / asset path problems → `UnsupportedLayoutItem`.
   511	- **`line`** — `at` (start, default `[0,0]`) and `to` (end), both in frame coordinates, either absolute
   512	  or edge-relative (§6); endpoints must differ **after** resolution. Plus `thickness` (> 0) and optional `when` gate. Lines have
   513	  no box `size`/`fit`/rotation. Endpoints must lie within the layout bounds: an out-of-bounds endpoint
   514	  is an error, not clipped. An absolute endpoint past `width.max` is rejected at load even on a
   515	  dynamic-width template, since no final width can bring it back inside.
   516	- **`container`** — a recursive group. Fields: placement (size defaults to `auto`/`auto` = fill parent),
   517	  optional `when` gate (§5), optional `frame` (`thickness` > 0, `rounded` bool), `padding`, and
   518	  `items` (nested layout). Children are positioned relative to the container's padded inner box.
   519	  `padding` is either a single number (uniform) or `[top, right, bottom, left]`; values must be ≥ 0;
   520	  default `0`.
   521	
   522	A data-bound `image` item's `name` must be unique and non-empty within a sibling list. `text` and `qr`
   523	items use `value` expressions and are anonymous.
   524

   569	## 6. Coordinate system
   570	
   571	All template coordinates use a **bottom-left origin with y pointing up**, expressed in the template's
   572	`unit`. Typst uses a top-left origin, so the renderer converts every placement with
   573	`dy = frame_height - (y + height)`. A `container` establishes a new coordinate frame: its children are
   574	measured against the container's **padded inner** width/height, not the page.
   575	
   576	When changing placement math, this conversion and the per-container reframing are the two things to get
   577	right.
   578	
   579	A coordinate component may be **edge-relative**: a *sign-negative* value is measured inward from the
   580	frame's far edge rather than outward from its origin.
   581	
   582	| Component | Sign | Measured from |
   583	| --- | --- | --- |
   584	| `x` | non-negative | left edge |
   585	| `x` | sign-negative | **right** edge: `frame_width + x` |
   586	| `y` | non-negative | bottom edge |
   587	| `y` | sign-negative | **top** edge: `frame_height + y` |
   588	
   589	`-0.0` (or `-0`) is the far edge exactly; `-2.0` is 2 units inside it. The test is the sign bit, not
   590	`< 0`, because `-0.0 < 0.0` is false. Edge-relative components apply to `at` and to `line`/box `to`,
   591	never to `size`, `max_w`, `max_h`, `padding`, or `thickness`, and they resolve against the **current**
   592	frame: the page, a container's padded inner box, or a rotated container's swapped author canvas.
   593	
   594	On a dynamic-width `single` the final width is not known until the measure pass runs, so an
   595	edge-relative `x` is bounds-checked against `width.max` at load time and re-checked at render. An
   596	edge-relative coordinate never contributes a frame-dependent width to the measured content extent, but
   597	an item that sizes itself to its content still contributes that content: a text box spanning to the
   598	right edge is measured at its natural width, so several full-width centered lines size the label to the
   599	longest of them. An edge-relative coordinate contributes its **inset** — the narrowest label it fits
   600	on — and that rule is the same for a box's `at.x` and for either endpoint of a `line`. A right-anchored
   601	`at.x` cannot be combined with an `auto` or frame-dependent width on a dynamic-width template.
   602	
   603	A `to`-sized `qr` or `image` is the exception: neither item type has an intrinsic content width the
   604	engine measures (see §4.1), so a frame-dependent `to` on either contributes nothing to the measured
   605	extent and the item simply stretches to whatever width the label resolves to. On a dynamic-width label
   606	where no other item sizes the content, the width falls back to `width.min`, which is enough for the
   607	item only when its own `at.x` fits inside that fallback; anchored further right than `width.min` the
   608	box resolves to a negative width and the render fails with `UnsupportedLayoutItem`. Intrinsic qr/image
   609	content sizing is deferred; see [#149](https://github.com/pfa230/labeler/issues/149).
   610	
   611	A box whose extent resolves to exactly **zero** is not an error at render: an empty data value can
   612	measure to precisely the item's own `at.x`, collapsing a `to`-spanning box to zero width, and a blank
   613	optional field is ordinary input rather than an authoring mistake. A **negative** extent is still an
   614	error, and load-time validation (which resolves against the `width.max` frame) still rejects corners
   615	that are inverted or degenerate there.
   616

   413	/// Render a single representative label to PNG. For sheets, renders one slot.
   414	pub fn render_thumbnail_png(
   415	    template: &TemplateDefinition,
   416	    data: &HashMap<String, JsonValue>,
   417	    option: Option<&BTreeMap<String, String>>,
   418	    settings: &BTreeMap<String, String>,
   419	    datetime: &crate::datetime_fmt::DateTimeResolver,
   420	) -> Result<Vec<u8>, AppError> {
   421	    let env = RenderEnv { settings, datetime };
   422	    let doc = compile_label_doc(template, data, option, &env)?;
   423	    let page = doc.pages().first().ok_or_else(|| {
   424	        AppError::render_failed(Reason::TypstNoPages, "typst did not produce any pages")
   425	    })?;
   426	    let pixmap = typst_render::render(page, &render_options(template.dpi as f32 / 72.0));

   428	        AppError::render_failed(
   429	            Reason::PngEncodeFailed,
   430	            format!("failed to encode png: {err}"),
   431	        )
   432	    })
   433	}

   850	    /// Resolve a template position against this frame, edge-relative components included. Errors if
   851	    /// either axis resolves below zero: compile time cannot always prove this on a dynamic-width
   852	    /// label, since an edge-relative inset is only checked against `width.max` at load time.
   853	    fn resolve_point(&self, p: &Position) -> Result<Point, AppError> {
   854	        const EPS: f32 = 1.0e-4;
   855	        let x = resolve_coord(p.x(), self.frame_width_units);
   856	        let y = resolve_coord(p.y(), self.frame_height_units);
   857	        if x < -EPS || y < -EPS {
   858	            return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
   859	                Reason::CoordOutOfFrame,
   860	                format!(
   861	                    "a coordinate resolves outside the frame: [{}, {}] against {}x{}",
   862	                    p.x(),
   863	                    p.y(),
   864	                    self.frame_width_units,
   865	                    self.frame_height_units
   866	                ),
   867	            ));
   868	        }
   869	        Ok(Point { x, y })
   870	    }

   872	    /// Mirrors `templates::validate_bounds` for the cases compile time had to defer.
   873	    fn check_box_bounds(&self, point: &Point, width: f32, height: f32) -> Result<(), AppError> {
   874	        const EPS: f32 = 1.0e-4;
   875	        if point.x + width > self.frame_width_units + EPS
   876	            || point.y + height > self.frame_height_units + EPS
   877	        {
   878	            return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
   879	                Reason::ItemOutOfFrame,
   880	                format!(
   881	                    "an item resolves outside the frame: {width}x{height} at [{}, {}] in {}x{}",
   882	                    point.x, point.y, self.frame_width_units, self.frame_height_units
   883	                ),
   884	            ));
   885	        }
   886	        Ok(())
   887	    }

   889	    /// The line checks compile time had to defer: on a dynamic-width label an edge-relative
   890	    /// endpoint is resolved against `max`, so neither its upper bound nor its degeneracy against a
   891	    /// plain endpoint could be decided until the final width was known.
   892	    fn check_line(&self, start: &Point, end: &Point) -> Result<(), AppError> {
   893	        const EPS: f32 = 1.0e-4;
   894	        for p in [start, end] {
   895	            if p.x > self.frame_width_units + EPS || p.y > self.frame_height_units + EPS {
   896	                return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
   897	                    Reason::LineEndpointOutOfFrame,
   898	                    format!(
   899	                        "a line endpoint resolves outside the frame: [{}, {}] in {}x{}",
   900	                        p.x, p.y, self.frame_width_units, self.frame_height_units
   901	                    ),
   902	                ));
   903	            }
   904	        }
   905	        if (start.x - end.x).abs() < EPS && (start.y - end.y).abs() < EPS {
   906	            return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
   907	                Reason::LineDegenerate,
   908	                "line start and end must differ after resolution",
   909	            ));
   910	        }
   911	        Ok(())
   912	    }

   914	    /// Walk items computing content right-extent and recording auto-width text fits (pre-order).
   915	    /// `budget_w` is the available width: page max at the top frame, inner width inside a container.
   916	    fn measure(
   917	        &self,
   918	        items: &[LayoutItem],
   919	        budget_w: f32,
   920	        out: &mut Vec<MeasuredText>,
   921	    ) -> Result<f32, AppError> {
   922	        let mut extent = 0.0f32;
   923	        for item in items {
   924	            if !self.is_item_active(item) {
   925	                continue;
   926	            }
   927	            // Clause 1: a right-anchored item cannot define the width it is anchored to. Its inset
   928	            // is the narrowest label it fits on, and that is all it can say. Skipping the item here
   929	            // also means it pushes no MeasuredText, which `render_text_item` must mirror exactly.
   930	            if let Some(at) = item_anchor(item) {
   931	                if at.x().is_sign_negative() {
   932	                    extent = extent.max(-at.x());
   933	                    continue;
   934	                }
   935	            }
   936	            // Clause 1, container case: unlike a leaf item, a right-anchored container's *inner
   937	            // frame* is not itself right-anchored. Its own width is always known here (see
   938	            // `item_anchor`'s doc comment), so its children's fits depend only on that known
   939	            // width, not on where the container ends up sitting. They must still be measured, or
   940	            // `render_container_item` (which recurses into every container unconditionally, with
   941	            // no clause-1 skip of its own) will consume `MeasuredText` entries this pass never
   942	            // pushed and fail with an auto-length cursor mismatch.
   943	            if let LayoutItem::Container {
   944	                placement,
   945	                padding,
   946	                items: children,
   947	                ..
   948	            } = item
   949	            {
   950	                if placement.at.x().is_sign_negative() {
   951	                    let at_y = resolve_coord(placement.at.y(), self.frame_height_units);
   952	                    self.measure_container_footprint(placement, at_y, padding, children, out)?;
   953	                    extent = extent.max(-placement.at.x());
   954	                    continue;
   955	                }
   956	            }
   957	            let right = match item {
   958	                LayoutItem::Text {
   959	                    value,
   960	                    placement,
   961	                    font_size,
   962	                    font_weight,
   963	                    multiline,
   964	                    alignment,
   965	                    ..
   966	                } => {
   967	                    let text = self.resolve_item_text(value)?;
   968	                    // Same weight the render pass will use: this pre-pass decides the auto width, so
   969	                    // measuring it unweighted would size the box for text that renders wider.
   970	                    let weight = match font_weight {
   971	                        Some(dyn_val) => resolve_dynamic_value_u16(dyn_val, self.data)?,
   972	                        None => 400,
   973	                    };
   974	                    // `at.y` may itself be edge-relative (measured from the top), so it must be
   975	                    // resolved before any height arithmetic. `at.x` is already known to be
   976	                    // non-negative here: clause 1 skipped the item otherwise.
   977	                    let at = Point {
   978	                        x: placement.at.x(),
   979	                        y: resolve_coord(placement.at.y(), self.frame_height_units),
   980	                    };
   981	                    if placement.width_is_frame_dependent() {
   982	                        // The right margin an edge-relative `to` asks for. Subtracting it from the
   983	                        // budget keeps this item's contribution (at.x + width + inset) inside
   984	                        // `budget_w`, so the clamp can never hand back a box narrower than the
   985	                        // width the text was wrapped at.
   986	                        let inset = match &placement.extent {
   987	                            Extent::To(to) if to.x().is_sign_negative() => -to.x(),
   988	                            _ => 0.0,
   989	                        };
   990	                        // The cap binds here, not at render: the rendered box for this item is
   991	                        // exactly `m.width`, so capping the budget is what caps the width.
   992	                        let budget = (budget_w - at.x - inset)
   993	                            .min(placement.max_w.unwrap_or(f32::INFINITY))
   994	                            .max(0.0);
   995	                        let box_h = self.measure_box_height(placement, at.y)?;
   996	                        let m = fit_text_auto_length(
   997	                            &text,
   998	                            font_size,
   999	                            *multiline,
  1000	                            weight,
  1001	                            alignment.vertical,
  1002	                            helpers::FitBox {
  1003	                                width_units: budget,
  1004	                                height_units: box_h,
  1005	                                unit: self.unit,
  1006	                            },
  1007	                        )?;
  1008	                        let w = m.width;
  1009	                        out.push(m);
  1010	                        at.x + w + inset
  1011	                    } else {
  1012	                        // Width only: this branch consumes just the width, and resolving the height
  1013	                        // here would let an axis measurement never uses decide whether the template
  1014	                        // renders. `measure_box_height` is the height-only counterpart.
  1015	                        let w = match &placement.extent {
  1016	                            // `None` here (unlike its three siblings) is safe only because this
  1017	                            // arm is reached exactly when `width_is_frame_dependent()` is false,
  1018	                            // which for `Extent::Size` means `size.0[0]` cannot be `auto` — so the
  1019	                            // `Auto` branch that would need a fallback is unreachable from here.
  1020	                            Extent::Size(size) => {
  1021	                                self.resolve_size_value(&size.0[0], placement.max_w, None, "width")?
  1022	                            }
  1023	                            Extent::To(_) => {
  1024	                                self.resolve_size(
  1025	                                    &placement.at,
  1026	                                    &placement.extent,
  1027	                                    placement.max_w,
  1028	                                    placement.max_h,
  1029	                                    false,
  1030	                                )?
  1031	                                .0
  1032	                            }
  1033	                        };
  1034	                        at.x + w
  1035	                    }

  1143	    /// A box item's vertical slot during measurement: its explicit height (capped by `max_h`, per
  1144	    /// #150/ADR-0053, so the slot this function returns can never exceed what `render_text_item`
  1145	    /// renders into), the box its corners describe, or the rest of the frame above `at_y` when
  1146	    /// neither an explicit height nor `max_h` is set. `at_y` is the **resolved** bottom edge, never
  1147	    /// the raw value: mixing a resolved `to.y` with a raw `at.y` inflates the slot.
  1148	    fn measure_box_height(&self, placement: &Placement, at_y: f32) -> Result<f32, AppError> {
  1149	        Ok(match &placement.extent {
  1150	            // The same call `render_text_item` makes for this slot, so the two cannot disagree
  1151	            // (#150). Only the height axis goes through the helper: `resolve_size` would also
  1152	            // resolve the width and error on a `size: [40, auto]` container, which must keep
  1153	            // measuring.
  1154	            Extent::Size(size) => self.resolve_size_value(
  1155	                &size.0[1],
  1156	                placement.max_h,
  1157	                Some((self.frame_height_units - at_y).max(0.0)),
  1158	                "height",
  1159	            )?,
  1160	            Extent::To(to) => resolve_coord(to.y(), self.frame_height_units) - at_y,
  1161	        })
  1162	    }

  1350	        // text off centre. Drop them at emission (#127); interior blanks are real spacing and stay.
  1351	        fn trim_blank_edges(lines: &[String]) -> Vec<String> {
  1352	            let start = lines.iter().position(|l| !l.trim().is_empty());
  1353	            let end = lines.iter().rposition(|l| !l.trim().is_empty());
  1354	            match (start, end) {
  1355	                (Some(s), Some(e)) => lines[s..=e].to_vec(),
  1356	                _ => Vec::new(),
  1357	            }
  1358	        }

  1380	                        placement.max_h,
  1381	                        Some((self.frame_height_units - point.y).max(0.0)),

  1383	                    )?,
  1384	                    Extent::To(_) => self.measure_box_height(placement, point.y)?,
  1385	                };
  1386	                let slot_top = self.frame_height_units - point.y - slot_h;
  1387	                // The box width is the resolved extent for a `to` (so a right-edge inset stays a
  1388	                // visible margin), and the content's fitted width for an auto size, matching what
  1389	                // the measure pass pushed.

  1418	                // the block inside it, exactly as the fixed-size path below does.
  1419	                // #124: pad the aligned edge so the ink Typst's cap-height/baseline line box leaves
  1420	                // outside — accents above, descenders below — lands inside the clipped slot.
  1421	                let body = pad_block(
  1422	                    &body,
  1423	                    helpers::pad_pt(weight, m.font, alignment.vertical)?,
  1424	                    alignment.vertical,
  1425	                );
  1426	                let inner = format!("#align({})[{body}]", typst_alignment(alignment));
  1427	                let dx = format_length(left, self.unit)?;
  1428	                let dy = format_length(slot_top, self.unit)?;
  1429	                let box_width = format_length(box_w, self.unit)?;
  1430	                let box_height = format_length(slot_h, self.unit)?;

  1432	                writeln!(
  1433	                    out,
  1434	                    "#place(top + left, dx: {dx}, dy: {dy})[#box(width: {box_width}, height: {box_height}, clip: true)[{content}]]"
  1435	                )
  1436	                .map_err(|err| {
  1437	                    AppError::render_failed(
  1438	                Reason::TypstSourceBuildFailed,
  1439	                format!("failed to build typst source: {err}"),
  1440	            )
  1441	                })?;
  1442	                return Ok(());
  1443	            }
  1444	        }
  1445	
  1446	        // `allow_auto_fill: true` matches text validation's `resolve_size` call in `templates.rs`:
  1447	        // text always has a frame to fall back on, on every format, not only when auto-length is
  1448	        // active (#155).
  1449	        let (width, box_height_units) = self.resolve_size(
  1450	            &placement.at,
  1451	            &placement.extent,
  1452	            placement.max_w,
  1453	            placement.max_h,

  1480	        let box_width = format_length(width, self.unit)?;
  1481	        let box_height = format_length(box_height_units, self.unit)?;

  1495	        )
  1496	        .map_err(|err| AppError::render_failed(
  1497	                Reason::TypstSourceBuildFailed,
  1498	                format!("failed to build typst source: {err}"),
  1499	            ))?;
  1500	
  1501	        Ok(())
  1502	    }

  1884	
  1885	    fn resolve_size(
  1886	        &self,
  1887	        at: &Position,
  1888	        extent: &Extent,
  1889	        max_w: Option<f32>,
  1890	        max_h: Option<f32>,
  1891	        allow_auto_fill: bool,
  1892	    ) -> Result<(f32, f32), AppError> {
  1893	        let size = match extent {
  1894	            Extent::Size(size) => size,
  1895	            Extent::To(to) => {
  1896	                return resolve_to_extent(at, to, self.frame_width_units, self.frame_height_units);
  1897	            }
  1898	        };
  1899	        let fallback = if allow_auto_fill {
  1900	            Some((
  1901	                (self.frame_width_units - resolve_coord(at.x(), self.frame_width_units)).max(0.0),
  1902	                (self.frame_height_units - resolve_coord(at.y(), self.frame_height_units)).max(0.0),
  1903	            ))
  1904	        } else {
  1905	            None
  1906	        };
  1907	        let width =
  1908	            self.resolve_size_value(&size.0[0], max_w, fallback.map(|value| value.0), "width")?;
  1909	        let height =
  1910	            self.resolve_size_value(&size.0[1], max_h, fallback.map(|value| value.1), "height")?;
  1911	        Ok((width, height))
  1912	    }
  1913	
  1914	    fn resolve_size_value(
  1915	        &self,
  1916	        value: &SizeValue,
  1917	        max: Option<f32>,
  1918	        fallback: Option<f32>,
  1919	        label: &str,
  1920	    ) -> Result<f32, AppError> {
  1921	        match value {
  1922	            SizeValue::Dynamic(dyn_val) => {
  1923	                let resolved = resolve_dynamic_value_f32(dyn_val, self.data)?;
  1924	                if resolved <= 0.0 {
  1925	                    return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
  1926	                        Reason::SizeInvalid,
  1927	                        format!("size {label} must be greater than 0"),
  1928	                    ));
  1929	                }
  1930	                Ok(resolved)
  1931	            }
  1932	            SizeValue::Auto(_) => {
  1933	                // `max_*` caps the resolution of `auto`; it does not replace the fallback. A cap
  1934	                // larger than the room available is simply not binding, so the smaller wins.
  1935	                let resolved = match (max, fallback) {
  1936	                    (Some(max), Some(fallback)) => max.min(fallback),
  1937	                    (Some(max), None) => max,
  1938	                    (None, Some(fallback)) => fallback,
  1939	                    (None, None) => {
  1940	                        return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
  1941	                            Reason::SizeAutoWithoutMax,
  1942	                            format!("size {label} is auto but no max_{label} provided"),
  1943	                        ))
  1944	                    }
  1945	                };
  1946	                if resolved <= 0.0 {
  1947	                    // Blame whichever of `max`/`fallback` is actually `resolved` (the smaller of
  1948	                    // the two, mirroring the `.min()` above): a `max_*` that isn't the binding
  1949	                    // value is fine even if it happens to be set, and a fallback of `0` (the
  1950	                    // anchor leaving no room) is not a `max_*` authoring error.
  1951	                    let max_is_binding = match (max, fallback) {
  1952	                        (Some(max), Some(fallback)) => max <= fallback,
  1953	                        (Some(_), None) => true,
  1954	                        (None, _) => false,
  1955	                    };
  1956	                    return Err(if max_is_binding {
  1957	                        AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
  1958	                            Reason::MaxSizeInvalid,
  1959	                            format!("max_{label} must be greater than 0"),
  1960	                        )
  1961	                    } else {
  1962	                        AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
  1963	                            Reason::SizeAutoNoRoom,
  1964	                            format!("no room left for an auto {label} at this anchor"),
  1965	                        )
  1966	                    });
  1967	                }
  1968	                Ok(resolved)
  1969	            }
  1970	        }
  1971	    }
  1972	
  1973	    fn wrap_rotation(&self, content: String, rotate: Option<f32>) -> String {
  1974	        // Typst positive angles rotate clockwise (screen coords); our `rotate` contract is
  1975	        // counter-clockwise, so negate. `reflow: true` normalizes the box to the rotated footprint.
  1976	        match rotate
  1977	            .and_then(Rotation::from_degrees)
  1978	            .unwrap_or(Rotation::R0)
  1979	        {
  1980	            Rotation::R0 => content,
  1981	            Rotation::R90 => format!("#rotate(-90deg, reflow: true)[{content}]"),
  1982	            Rotation::R180 => format!("#rotate(180deg, reflow: true)[{content}]"),
  1983	            Rotation::R270 => format!("#rotate(90deg, reflow: true)[{content}]"),
  1984	        }
  1985	    }

   294	pub(super) fn typst_font_options() -> TypstKitFontOptions {
   295	    let dir = crate::resolve_dir(std::env::var_os("LABELER_FONTS_DIR"), "fonts");
   296	    // Exclude host system fonts so render output depends only on the bundled fonts and is identical
   297	    // across dev, CI, and the deployed container; a system-installed face must never shadow the
   298	    // bundled Inter. See #100. (`include_embedded_fonts` stays on for Typst's default fallback faces.)
   299	    TypstKitFontOptions::default()
   300	        .include_system_fonts(false)
   301	        .include_dirs([dir])
   302	}
   303	
   304	/// The box a fit has to land inside, in template units. Grouped because the width, the height and
   305	/// the unit they are expressed in are one fact, and passing them as three parallel floats pushed the
   306	/// fitting entry points past a sane argument count.
   307	#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
   308	pub(super) struct FitBox<'a> {
   309	    pub width_units: f32,
   310	    pub height_units: f32,
   311	    pub unit: &'a str,
   312	}
   313	
   314	const WGHT: ttf_parser::Tag = ttf_parser::Tag::from_bytes(b"wght");
   315	const OPSZ: ttf_parser::Tag = ttf_parser::Tag::from_bytes(b"opsz");
   316	
   317	/// Parse a face and confirm it carries the axes the fitter varies. Byte-taking and free of the cache
   318	/// so a test can hand it any font without depending on which font some earlier test loaded first.
   319	fn load_face(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<ttf_parser::Face<'_>, AppError> {
   320	    let face = ttf_parser::Face::parse(bytes, 0).map_err(|err| {
   321	        AppError::render_failed(
   322	            Reason::FontParseFailed,
   323	            format!("failed to parse font: {err}"),
   324	        )
   325	    })?;
   326	    // `set_variation` reports success for any variable face even when no axis matches the tag, so it
   327	    // cannot serve as the check. Verify up front: a font without these axes would measure silently
   328	    // unweighted, which is the bug this measurement path exists to remove (#96).
   329	    for tag in [WGHT, OPSZ] {
   330	        if !face
   331	            .variation_axes()
   332	            .into_iter()
   333	            .any(|axis| axis.tag == tag)
   334	        {
   335	            return Err(AppError::render_failed(
   336	                Reason::FontAxisMissing,
   337	                format!("measurement font lacks the '{tag}' variation axis"),
   338	            ));
   339	        }
   340	    }
   341	    Ok(face)
   342	}
   343	
   344	fn font_bytes() -> Result<&'static [u8], AppError> {
   345	    static BYTES: OnceLock<Vec<u8>> = OnceLock::new();
   346	    if let Some(bytes) = BYTES.get() {
   347	        return Ok(bytes);
   348	    }
   349	    let path = crate::resolve_dir(std::env::var_os("LABELER_FONTS_DIR"), "fonts")
   350	        .join("InterVariable.ttf");
   351	    let bytes = std::fs::read(&path).map_err(|err| {
   352	        AppError::render_failed(
   353	            Reason::FontReadFailed,
   354	            format!("failed to read font: {err}"),
   355	        )
   356	    })?;
   357	    load_face(&bytes)?;
   358	    // A concurrent caller may win the race to populate the cache; either value is valid, so fall
   359	    // back to the stored bytes rather than treating the lost race as an error.
   360	    let _ = BYTES.set(bytes);
   361	    Ok(BYTES.get().expect("font bytes initialized"))
   362	}
   363	
   364	/// The face instanced the way Typst will render it: `wght` from the item's weight, `opsz` from the
   365	/// font size. Typst sets both automatically (typst-library `text/font/variations.rs`), and measuring
   366	/// the default instance instead is what made bold text overflow and large text shrink needlessly.
   367	/// Out-of-range values normalise against the axis, so this clamps as Typst's do.
   368	fn instance(weight: u16, size_pt: f32) -> Result<ttf_parser::Face<'static>, AppError> {
   369	    let mut face = load_face(font_bytes()?)?;
   370	    face.set_variation(WGHT, f32::from(weight));
   371	    face.set_variation(OPSZ, size_pt);
   372	    Ok(face)
   373	}
   374	
   375	/// Largest font in [min_size, max_size] (0.5pt steps) at which `text` fits the box, else min_size.
   376	pub(super) fn largest_fitting_font(
   377	    text: &str,
   378	    multiline: bool,
   379	    weight: u16,
   380	    vertical: VerticalAlign,
   381	    min_size: f32,
   382	    max_size: f32,
   383	    fit: FitBox,
   384	) -> f32 {
   385	    let width_pt = units_to_pt(fit.width_units, fit.unit);
   386	    let height_pt = units_to_pt(fit.height_units, fit.unit);
   387	    // Parse once, mutate per candidate: the loop runs up to ~76 times at 0.5pt steps, and
   388	    // `set_variation` only rewrites normalised coordinates.
   389	    let mut face = match instance(weight, max_size) {
   390	        Ok(face) => face,
   391	        Err(_) => return min_size,
   392	    };
   393	    let mut size = max_size;
   394	    while size >= min_size - f32::EPSILON {
   395	        // opsz tracks the size Typst would render this candidate at.
   396	        face.set_variation(OPSZ, size);
   397	        if text_fits(&face, text, multiline, size, width_pt, height_pt, vertical) {
   398	            return size;
   399	        }
   400	        size -= 0.5;
   401	    }
   402	    min_size
   403	}
   404	
   405	pub(super) fn fit_text_to_box(
   406	    text: &str,
   407	    multiline: bool,
   408	    weight: u16,
   409	    vertical: VerticalAlign,
   410	    min_size: f32,
   411	    max_size: f32,
   412	    fit: FitBox,
   413	) -> Result<(f32, String), AppError> {
   414	    let fitted = largest_fitting_font(text, multiline, weight, vertical, min_size, max_size, fit);
   415	    let face = instance(weight, fitted)?;
   416	    let face = &face;
   417	    let width_pt = units_to_pt(fit.width_units, fit.unit);
   418	    let height_pt = units_to_pt(fit.height_units, fit.unit);
   419	    if text_fits(face, text, multiline, fitted, width_pt, height_pt, vertical) {
   420	        if multiline {
   421	            let lines = wrap_text(face, text, fitted, width_pt);
   422	            return Ok((fitted, lines.join("\n")));
   423	        }
   424	        return Ok((fitted, text.to_string()));
   425	    }
   426	    let trimmed = if multiline {
   427	        trim_multiline(face, text, fitted, width_pt, height_pt, vertical)
   428	    } else {
   429	        trim_single_line(face, text, fitted, width_pt)
   430	    };
   431	    Ok((fitted, trimmed))
   432	}
   433	
   434	fn text_fits(
   435	    face: &ttf_parser::Face,
   436	    text: &str,
   437	    multiline: bool,
   438	    size: f32,
   439	    width_pt: f32,
   440	    height_pt: f32,
   441	    vertical: VerticalAlign,
   442	) -> bool {
   443	    if multiline {
   444	        let lines = wrap_text(face, text, size, width_pt);
   445	        block_height(face, size, lines.len(), vertical) <= height_pt + 0.01
   446	    } else {
   447	        text_width(face, text, size) <= width_pt + 0.01
   448	            && block_height(face, size, 1, vertical) <= height_pt + 0.01
   449	    }
   450	}
   451	
   452	fn trim_single_line(face: &ttf_parser::Face, text: &str, size: f32, width_pt: f32) -> String {
   453	    const ELLIPSIS: &str = "...";
   454	    let mut out = text.to_string();
   455	    if text_width(face, &out, size) <= width_pt {
   456	        return out;
   457	    }
   458	    let ellipsis_width = text_width(face, ELLIPSIS, size);
   459	    if ellipsis_width > width_pt {
   460	        return ELLIPSIS.to_string();
   461	    }
   462	    while !out.is_empty() && text_width(face, &format!("{out}{ELLIPSIS}"), size) > width_pt {
   463	        out.pop();
   464	    }
   465	    format!("{out}{ELLIPSIS}")
   466	}
   467	
   468	fn trim_multiline(
   469	    face: &ttf_parser::Face,
   470	    text: &str,
   471	    size: f32,
   472	    width_pt: f32,
   473	    height_pt: f32,
   474	    vertical: VerticalAlign,
   475	) -> String {
   476	    const ELLIPSIS: &str = "...";
   477	    // Inverse of block_height, with the edge reservation taken off the top:
   478	    // n*cap + (n-1)*leading <= H - overflow  =>  n <= (H - overflow + leading) / (cap + leading)
   479	    let max_lines = ((height_pt - overflow_em(face, vertical) * size + leading(size))
   480	        / (cap_height(face, size) + leading(size)))
   481	    .floor()
   482	    .max(1.0) as usize;
   483	    let mut lines = wrap_text(face, text, size, width_pt);
   484	    if lines.len() <= max_lines {
   485	        return lines.join("\n");
   486	    }
   487	    lines.truncate(max_lines);
   488	    let last = lines.last_mut().unwrap();
   489	    let ellipsis_width = text_width(face, ELLIPSIS, size);
   490	    if ellipsis_width > width_pt {
   491	        *last = ELLIPSIS.to_string();
   492	    } else {
   493	        while !last.is_empty() && text_width(face, &format!("{last}{ELLIPSIS}"), size) > width_pt {
   494	            last.pop();
   495	        }
   496	        *last = format!("{last}{ELLIPSIS}");
   497	    }
   498	    lines.join("\n")
   499	}
   500	
   501	fn wrap_text(face: &ttf_parser::Face, text: &str, size: f32, width_pt: f32) -> Vec<String> {
   502	    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   503	    let space_width = text_width(face, " ", size);
   504	    let paragraphs: Vec<&str> = text.split('\n').collect();
   505	    for paragraph in paragraphs {
   506	        if paragraph.is_empty() {
   507	            lines.push(String::new());
   508	            continue;
   509	        }
   510	        let mut current = String::new();
   511	        let mut current_width = 0.0;
   512	        for word in paragraph.split_whitespace() {
   513	            let word_width = text_width(face, word, size);
   514	            if current.is_empty() {
   515	                if word_width <= width_pt {
   516	                    current.push_str(word);
   517	                    current_width = word_width;
   518	                } else {
   519	                    let mut chunk = String::new();
   520	                    let mut chunk_width = 0.0;
   521	                    for ch in word.chars() {
   522	                        let ch_width = text_width(face, &ch.to_string(), size);
   523	                        if !chunk.is_empty() && chunk_width + ch_width > width_pt {
   524	                            lines.push(chunk);
   525	                            chunk = String::new();
   526	                            chunk_width = 0.0;
   527	                        }
   528	                        chunk.push(ch);
   529	                        chunk_width += ch_width;
   530	                    }
   531	                    if !chunk.is_empty() {
   532	                        current = chunk;
   533	                        current_width = chunk_width;
   534	                    }
   535	                }
   536	                continue;
   537	            }
   538	
   539	            if current_width + space_width + word_width <= width_pt {
   540	                current.push(' ');
   541	                current.push_str(word);
   542	                current_width += space_width + word_width;
   543	            } else {
   544	                lines.push(current);
   545	                current = String::new();
   546	                if word_width <= width_pt {
   547	                    current.push_str(word);
   548	                    current_width = word_width;
   549	                } else {
   550	                    let mut chunk = String::new();
   551	                    let mut chunk_width = 0.0;
   552	                    for ch in word.chars() {
   553	                        let ch_width = text_width(face, &ch.to_string(), size);
   554	                        if !chunk.is_empty() && chunk_width + ch_width > width_pt {
   555	                            lines.push(chunk);
   556	                            chunk = String::new();
   557	                            chunk_width = 0.0;
   558	                        }
   559	                        chunk.push(ch);
   560	                        chunk_width += ch_width;
   561	                    }
   562	                    current = chunk;
   563	                    current_width = chunk_width;
   564	                }
   565	            }
   566	        }
   567	        if !current.is_empty() {
   568	            lines.push(current);
   569	        }
   570	    }
   571	    lines
   572	}
   573	
   574	fn text_width(face: &ttf_parser::Face, text: &str, size: f32) -> f32 {
   575	    let upem = f32::from(face.units_per_em());
   576	    text.chars()
   577	        .map(|ch| {
   578	            // A character Inter lacks measures as .notdef, which is what fontdue did. Typst renders
   579	            // it from a fallback face, so it is not an error here — only an approximation, as before.
   580	            // Dropping it instead would measure zero width, and under-measuring is what overflows.
   581	            let glyph = face.glyph_index(ch).unwrap_or(ttf_parser::GlyphId(0));
   582	            f32::from(face.glyph_hor_advance(glyph).unwrap_or(0))
   583	        })
   584	        .sum::<f32>()
   585	        / upem
   586	        * size
   587	}
   588	
   589	/// Typst reads the typographic (OS/2 sTypo*) metrics and falls back to hhea; match it, or every
   590	/// derived number is measured against a font the renderer is not using
   591	/// (typst-library `text/font/metrics.rs`).
   592	fn typo_ascender(face: &ttf_parser::Face) -> f32 {
   593	    f32::from(
   594	        face.typographic_ascender()
   595	            .unwrap_or_else(|| face.ascender()),
   596	    )
   597	}
   598	
   599	fn typo_descender(face: &ttf_parser::Face) -> f32 {
   600	    f32::from(
   601	        face.typographic_descender()
   602	            .unwrap_or_else(|| face.descender()),
   603	    )
   604	}
   605	
   606	/// Typst's line box runs cap-height to baseline (`text/mod.rs` top/bottom edge defaults).
   607	fn cap_height(face: &ttf_parser::Face, size: f32) -> f32 {
   608	    let upem = f32::from(face.units_per_em());
   609	    // Falls back to the *typographic* ascender, as Typst does, not the hhea one. Only differs for a
   610	    // font supplied through LABELER_FONTS_DIR, which is exactly what the bundled-font tests cannot see.
   611	    let cap = face
   612	        .capital_height()
   613	        .filter(|v| *v > 0)
   614	        .map(f32::from)
   615	        .unwrap_or_else(|| typo_ascender(face));
   616	    cap / upem * size
   617	}
   618	
   619	/// Ink above the cap-height line, as a fraction of the em: where accents live.
   620	fn ascent_overflow_em(face: &ttf_parser::Face) -> f32 {

 succeeded in 0ms:
openspec/specs/.gitkeep
openspec/specs/template-registry/spec.md
     1	# template-registry Specification
     2	
     3	## Purpose
     4	Defines how the templates directory is turned into the served template registry: which files are read, what happens to a file whose content is unusable, how two files claiming one id resolve, and how the refused files are reported to the operator. The registry is rebuilt from disk by this same contract at startup and on every reload.
     5	
     6	## Requirements
     7	
     8	### Requirement: Startup survives every template content fault
     9	
    10	The service SHALL start whenever the templates directory is readable, regardless of the content of the files in it. A file that fails to parse, fails validation, or claims an id another file already holds SHALL be excluded from the served set and reported as broken, and SHALL NOT abort startup.
    11	
    12	The service SHALL abort startup only when the templates directory itself cannot be read or enumerated, or an individual file cannot be read.
    13	
    14	This requirement supersedes the `docs/SPEC.md` §3 sentence "Parsing rejects unknown fields (`deny_unknown_fields`). An invalid template aborts server startup." for its second sentence only; unknown-field rejection is unchanged.
    15	
    16	#### Scenario: A malformed file does not stop the service
    17	
    18	- **WHEN** the templates directory holds one valid template and one file that is not valid template YAML
    19	- **THEN** the service starts
    20	- **AND** the valid template is served
    21	- **AND** the malformed file is reported as broken
    22	
    23	#### Scenario: An unreadable templates directory is fatal
    24	
    25	- **WHEN** the templates directory cannot be read
    26	- **THEN** the service exits with a fatal error naming the directory
    27	
    28	### Requirement: Templates load in a deterministic order
    29	
    30	The registry SHALL load the files of the templates directory whose extension is `yaml` or `yml`, compared case-insensitively, in ascending byte order of filename, independent of the order the filesystem enumerates them. Files with any other extension SHALL be ignored.
    31	
    32	Two loads of the same directory contents SHALL therefore produce the same served set, the same id-to-file mapping, and the same broken list, on any machine.
    33	
    34	#### Scenario: Load result does not depend on filesystem order
    35	
    36	- **WHEN** the same set of template files is loaded on two machines whose directory enumeration order differs
    37	- **THEN** both loads serve the same templates from the same files
    38	- **AND** both report the same broken files
    39	
    40	### Requirement: A duplicate template id refuses the colliding file
    41	
    42	When two or more files in the templates directory declare the same template `id`, the registry SHALL serve the one whose filename sorts first in byte order (so `Z.yaml` sorts before `a.yaml` on a case-sensitive filesystem) and SHALL refuse each later file for that id.
    43	
    44	A refused file SHALL:
    45	
    46	- be excluded from the served set, leaving the winning file's template served and unchanged;
    47	- appear in the broken list, identified by its own filename;
    48	- carry an error message naming the duplicated id, and the filename of the file it collides with.
    49	
    50	Refusing a colliding file SHALL NOT eject, alter, or invalidate the template already accepted for that id, and SHALL NOT affect any other template in the directory.
    51	
    52	A duplicate id SHALL NOT produce an HTTP error response. The `template_duplicate_id` reason of `docs/SPEC.md` §10.1 therefore no longer reaches the wire: it names the cause carried in a `broken` entry's message, and no request can provoke a `TemplateInvalid` response carrying it.
    53	
    54	This requirement supersedes the `docs/SPEC.md` §3 top-level field table entry for `id` ("Required, non-empty, unique across the directory") to the extent of what a collision does: the id remains required and non-empty, and uniqueness is now enforced by refusing the colliding file rather than by refusing to run. It also supersedes the §10.1 row for `template_duplicate_id` as to where that reason appears.
    55	
    56	#### Scenario: Two files declare one id
    57	
    58	- **WHEN** `alpha.yaml` and `zed.yaml` both declare `id: badge` and both are otherwise valid
    59	- **THEN** the service starts
    60	- **AND** `badge` is served from `alpha.yaml`
    61	- **AND** `zed.yaml` is reported as broken with a message naming `badge` and `alpha.yaml`
    62	- **AND** every other template in the directory is served normally
    63	
    64	#### Scenario: A colliding file cannot evict the served template
    65	
    66	- **WHEN** a directory serving `badge` from `alpha.yaml` gains `zed.yaml`, which also declares `id: badge`, and the registry is reloaded
    67	- **THEN** `badge` is still served from `alpha.yaml`
    68	- **AND** `zed.yaml` is reported as broken
    69	
    70	#### Scenario: A collision resolves once the operator fixes it
    71	
    72	- **WHEN** the operator changes the id in the refused file, renames it, or deletes it, and reloads the templates
    73	- **THEN** the collision is gone from the broken list
    74	- **AND** the surviving files are served
    75	
    76	### Requirement: Refused templates are reported, not silently dropped
    77	
    78	Every file the registry refuses SHALL be reported through all three of these channels:
    79	
    80	- a warning log line at startup, carrying the file and the reason it was refused;
    81	- the `broken` list of `GET /api/templates`, as `{ filename, error }` entries carrying that same
    82	  reason, omitted when empty;
    83	- the `broken_count` of the `POST /api/templates/reload` response, alongside `count`, counting the
    84	  refused files.
    85	
    86	`POST /api/templates/reload` SHALL succeed and swap in the new registry whenever the templates directory is readable, including when some files were refused. It SHALL NOT fail on refused files.
    87	
    88	This requirement supersedes the `docs/SPEC.md` §2.0 bullet "`POST /templates/reload` re-scans the dir and returns `{ "count": N }`", and the §2.0 sentence "A reload that fails (an invalid file on disk) returns `422` and keeps the previously-loaded set, so a bad file never takes the service down." A reload now fails only when the directory or one of its files cannot be read; it then returns `500 RenderFailed` with `details.reason` `template_registry_io` and keeps the previously-loaded set.
    89	
    90	#### Scenario: Reload reports the collision instead of failing
    91	
    92	- **WHEN** a duplicate id is created on disk and `POST /api/templates/reload` is called
    93	- **THEN** the response is `200` with `count` for the served templates and `broken_count` including the refused file
    94	- **AND** `GET /api/templates` lists the refused file under `broken` with its message
    95	
    96	#### Scenario: Reload keeps the live set when the directory is unreadable
    97	
    98	- **WHEN** the templates directory cannot be read and `POST /api/templates/reload` is called
    99	- **THEN** the request returns `500 RenderFailed` with `details.reason` `template_registry_io`
   100	- **AND** the previously-loaded templates stay served
   101	
   102	### Requirement: A `422` from a template write means nothing was written
   103	
   104	`POST /api/templates` and `PUT /api/templates/{id}` SHALL return `422 TemplateInvalid` only for a submitted body that fails parsing or validation, which is rejected before anything is written. The directory re-read that follows a successful write SHALL NOT produce a `422`: the only fault it can still raise is an unreadable directory or file, which is a `500`, and the file stays written.
   105	
   106	`DELETE /api/templates/{id}` SHALL NOT return `422`. A refused file elsewhere in the directory SHALL NOT block a delete.
   107	
   108	This requirement supersedes the `docs/SPEC.md` §2.0 `PUT /templates/{id}` bullet's account of a `422` having two sources with different effects, including "Callers must not read a `422` as 'nothing was saved'", and the §2.0 `DELETE /templates/{id}` bullet's `422` clause. It also supersedes the §2 endpoint table's success column for `POST /templates/reload` (`200 {"count":N}` / `422`), which now reads `200 { "count": N, "broken_count": N }` / `500`.
   109	
   110	#### Scenario: An invalid body is rejected before the write
   111	
   112	- **WHEN** `PUT /api/templates/{id}` receives a body that fails validation
   113	- **THEN** the response is `422`
   114	- **AND** the stored file is unchanged
   115	
   116	#### Scenario: A refused sibling does not block a delete
   117	
   118	- **WHEN** `DELETE /api/templates/{id}` is called on a served template while another file in the directory is refused
   119	- **THEN** the response is `204`
   120	- **AND** the refused file is still reported as broken

   351	    pub fn param_ref(name: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
   352	        SizeValue::Dynamic(DynamicValue::Ref(name.into()))
   353	    }
   354	
   355	    pub fn auto() -> Self {
   356	        SizeValue::Auto(AutoSize::Auto)
   357	    }
   358	}
   359	
   360	impl From<f32> for SizeValue {
   361	    fn from(value: f32) -> Self {
   362	        SizeValue::Dynamic(DynamicValue::Literal(value))
   363	    }
   364	}
   365	
   366	impl From<DynamicValue<f32>> for SizeValue {
   367	    fn from(value: DynamicValue<f32>) -> Self {
   368	        SizeValue::Dynamic(value)
   369	    }
   370	}
   371	
   372	#[derive(Debug, Serialize, ToSchema, Clone, Deserialize, PartialEq)]
   373	#[serde(transparent)]
   374	pub struct Size(pub [SizeValue; 2]);
   375	
   376	/// Orthogonal rotation interpreted from the wire `rotate` degrees (counter-clockwise).
   377	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   378	pub enum Rotation {
   379	    R0,
   380	    R90,
   381	    R180,
   382	    R270,
   383	}
   384	
   385	impl Rotation {
   386	    /// Canonicalize wire degrees to an orthogonal rotation. `None` for non-finite or
   387	    /// non-multiple-of-90 (within `EPS`). Handles negatives and >360 via `rem_euclid`.
   388	    pub fn from_degrees(deg: f32) -> Option<Rotation> {
   389	        if !deg.is_finite() {
   390	            return None;
   391	        }
   392	        const EPS: f32 = 1.0e-3;
   393	        let norm = deg.rem_euclid(360.0);
   394	        for (target, rot) in [
   395	            (0.0, Rotation::R0),
   396	            (90.0, Rotation::R90),
   397	            (180.0, Rotation::R180),
   398	            (270.0, Rotation::R270),
   399	            (360.0, Rotation::R0),
   400	        ] {
   401	            if (norm - target).abs() < EPS {
   402	                return Some(rot);
   403	            }
   404	        }
   405	        None
   406	    }
   407	
   408	    /// 90/270 swap width and height.
   409	    pub fn swaps_axes(self) -> bool {
   410	        matches!(self, Rotation::R90 | Rotation::R270)
   411	    }
   412	
   413	    /// Anything other than `R0` triggers the rotated render/validation path.
   414	    pub fn is_rotated(self) -> bool {
   415	        !matches!(self, Rotation::R0)
   416	    }
   417	}
   418	
   419	/// How a box item's extent is expressed on the wire: `size:` (width and height) xor `to:` (the
   420	/// opposite corner). An enum rather than two `Option`s so "exactly one" is a type invariant.
   421	#[derive(Debug, Serialize, ToSchema, Clone)]
   422	#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
   423	pub enum Extent {
   424	    Size(Size),
   425	    To(Position),
   426	}
   427	
   428	#[derive(Debug, Serialize, ToSchema, Clone)]
   429	pub struct Placement {
   430	    #[serde(default)]
   431	    pub at: Position,
   432	    #[serde(flatten)]
   433	    pub extent: Extent,
   434	    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
   435	    pub max_w: Option<f32>,
   436	    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
   437	    pub max_h: Option<f32>,
   438	    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
   439	    pub rotate: Option<f32>,
   440	}
   441	
   442	impl Placement {
   443	    /// The common case: an `at`/`size` placement with no bounds or rotation.
   444	    pub fn sized(at: Position, size: Size) -> Self {
   445	        Self {
   446	            at,
   447	            extent: Extent::Size(size),
   448	            max_w: None,
   449	            max_h: None,
   450	            rotate: None,
   451	        }
   452	    }
   453	
   454	    /// The `Size` when the extent is expressed that way, for the `auto`-aware paths.
   455	    pub fn size_or_auto(&self) -> Option<&Size> {
   456	        match &self.extent {
   457	            Extent::Size(size) => Some(size),
   458	            Extent::To(_) => None,
   459	        }
   460	    }

   271	/// renders one slot at label_width/label_height. Shared by `compile_single_doc` (after its
   272	/// Single-only guard) and the thumbnail path.
   273	fn compile_label_doc(
   274	    template: &TemplateDefinition,
   275	    data: &HashMap<String, JsonValue>,
   276	    option: Option<&BTreeMap<String, String>>,
   277	    env: &RenderEnv,
   278	) -> Result<PagedDocument, AppError> {

   280	    let selected_option = normalize_option(template, option)?;

   309	    let cursor_cell: Cell<usize>;
   310

   313	        ..

   320	            .ok_or_else(|| AppError::unsupported_format("dynamic single width requires max"))?;

   325	            .ok_or_else(|| AppError::unsupported_format("dynamic single width requires min"))?;
   326	
   327	        check_dimension_limit(min_w, unit, max_dim_mm, "width min")?;

   676	/// Count rendered PDF pages by counting "/Type /Page" objects (excluding the "/Type /Pages" tree
   677	/// node). Used by pagination tests.
   678	pub fn count_pdf_pages(pdf: &[u8]) -> usize {
   679	    // typst-pdf 0.15 serializes dictionary keys without whitespace (`/Type/Page`, `/Type/Pages`).
   680	    let needle = b"/Type/Page";
   681	    let mut count = 0usize;
   682	    let mut i = 0;
   683	    while let Some(pos) = pdf[i..].windows(needle.len()).position(|w| w == needle) {
   684	        let at = i + pos;
   685	        let after = at + needle.len();
   686	        if pdf.get(after) != Some(&b's') {
   687	            count += 1;
   688	        }
   689	        i = after;
   690	    }

   763	/// `size = to - at`, both corners resolved against the frame first: `to` is the top-right corner of
   764	/// a box whose bottom-left is `at`. Mirrors `templates::resolve_to_extent` (kept in sync per this
   765	/// file's compile-time/render-time note) with one deliberate difference: a *zero* extent is legal
   766	/// here and rejected there. On a dynamic-width label an empty data value can measure to exactly
   767	/// `at.x`, so the label clamps to it and a `to`-spanning box collapses to zero width. That is an
   768	/// ordinary outcome of blank input, not an authoring mistake; a *negative* extent still is one, and
   769	/// the compile-time check (which resolves against the max-width frame) still rejects corners that
   770	/// are statically inverted or degenerate.
   771	fn resolve_to_extent(
   772	    at: &Position,
   773	    to: &Position,
   774	    frame_w: f32,
   775	    frame_h: f32,
   776	) -> Result<(f32, f32), AppError> {
   777	    const EPS: f32 = 1.0e-4;
   778	    let width = resolve_coord(to.x(), frame_w) - resolve_coord(at.x(), frame_w);
   779	    let height = resolve_coord(to.y(), frame_h) - resolve_coord(at.y(), frame_h);
   780	    if width < -EPS || height < -EPS {
   781	        return Err(AppError::unsupported_layout_item(
   782	            Reason::EdgeRectInverted,
   783	            "to must be above and to the right of at",
   784	        ));
   785	    }
   786	    Ok((width.max(0.0), height.max(0.0)))
   787	}
   788	
   789	struct RenderContext<'a> {
   790	    frame_width_units: f32,
   791	    frame_height_units: f32,
   792	    unit: &'a str,
   793	    data: &'a HashMap<String, JsonValue>,
   794	    selected_option: Option<&'a BTreeMap<String, String>>,
   795	    env: &'a RenderEnv<'a>,
   796	    images: &'a RefCell<ImageCollector>,
   797	    mode: LengthMode<'a>,
   798	}
   799	
   800	impl<'a> RenderContext<'a> {
   801	    fn new(
   802	        frame: (f32, f32),
   803	        unit: &'a str,
   804	        data: &'a HashMap<String, JsonValue>,
   805	        selected_option: Option<&'a BTreeMap<String, String>>,
   806	        env: &'a RenderEnv<'a>,
   807	        images: &'a RefCell<ImageCollector>,
   808	        mode: LengthMode<'a>,
   809	    ) -> Self {
   810	        Self {
   811	            frame_width_units: frame.0,
   812	            frame_height_units: frame.1,
   813	            unit,
   814	            data,
   815	            selected_option,
   816	            env,
   817	            images,
   818	            mode,
   819	        }
   820	    }
   821	
   822	    fn is_dynamic_width(&self) -> bool {
   823	        matches!(self.mode, LengthMode::Dynamic(_))
   824	    }
   825	
   826	    fn is_item_active(&self, item: &LayoutItem) -> bool {
   827	        if let Some(when) = item.when() {
   828	            when.iter().all(|(param_name, expected_val)| {
   829	                if let Some(val) = self.data.get(param_name) {
   830	                    let val_str = value_to_string(val);
   831	                    &val_str == expected_val
   832	                } else if let Some(selected) = self.selected_option {
   833	                    selected.get(param_name) == Some(expected_val)
   834	                } else {
   835	                    false
   836	                }
   837	            })
   838	        } else {
   839	            true
   840	        }
   841	    }
   842	
   843	    fn auto_length(&self) -> Option<&AutoLength<'a>> {
   844	        match &self.mode {
   845	            LengthMode::Dynamic(al) => Some(al),
   846	            LengthMode::Fixed => None,
   847	        }
   848	    }

   280	}
   281	
   282	/// Resolve a template coordinate against the frame it is placed in. A **sign-negative** value is
   283	/// measured inward from the frame's far edge: `-0.0` is the edge itself, `-2.0` is 2 units inside
   284	/// it. The test is the sign bit and not `< 0.0`, because `-0.0 < 0.0` is false and `-0.0` is how a
   285	/// template spells "the far edge". Total by design: a result below zero is a validation error the
   286	/// caller raises, not something this function can decide.
   287	pub fn resolve_coord(v: f32, frame_extent: f32) -> f32 {
   288	    if v.is_sign_negative() {
   289	        frame_extent + v
   290	    } else {
   291	        v
   292	    }
   293	}
   294

   310	        }

   314	        self.0[0]
   315	    }
   316	
   317	    pub fn y(&self) -> f32 {
   318	        self.0[1]
   319	    }
   320	}

   629	
   630	fn resolve_f32_default(params: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, ParamSpec>, name: &str) -> f32 {
   631	    if let Some(spec) = params.get(name) {
   632	        match &spec.default {
   633	            Some(crate::models::ParamValue::Float(f)) => *f,
   634	            Some(crate::models::ParamValue::Integer(i)) => *i as f32,
   635	            Some(crate::models::ParamValue::String(s)) => {
   636	                s.parse::<f32>().unwrap_or_else(|_| spec.min.unwrap_or(0.0))
   637	            }
   638	            _ => spec.min.unwrap_or(0.0),
   639	        }
   640	    } else {
   641	        0.0
   642	    }
   643	}
   644	
   645	fn resolve_u16_default(params: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, ParamSpec>, name: &str) -> u16 {
   646	    if let Some(spec) = params.get(name) {
   647	        match &spec.default {
   648	            Some(crate::models::ParamValue::Integer(i)) => *i as u16,
   649	            Some(crate::models::ParamValue::Float(f)) => *f as u16,
   650	            _ => 400,
   651	        }
   652	    } else {
   653	        400
   654	    }
   655	}
   656	
   657	fn instantiate_format_defaults(
   658	    format: &TemplateFormat,
   659	    params: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, ParamSpec>,
   660	) -> TemplateFormat {
   661	    match format {
   662	        TemplateFormat::Single {
   663	            width,
   664	            height,
   665	            media_width,
   666	        } => {
   667	            let inst_dim = |dim: &DynamicDimension| -> DynamicDimension {
   668	                match dim {
   669	                    DynamicDimension::Fixed(dv) => {
   670	                        let val = match dv {
   671	                            DynamicValue::Literal(v) => *v,
   672	                            DynamicValue::Ref(r) => resolve_f32_default(params, r),
   673	                        };
   674	                        DynamicDimension::Fixed(DynamicValue::Literal(val))
   675	                    }
   676	                    DynamicDimension::Dynamic { min, max } => {
   677	                        let inst_dv =
   678	                            |dv: &Option<DynamicValue<f32>>| -> Option<DynamicValue<f32>> {
   679	                                dv.as_ref().map(|v| match v {
   680	                                    DynamicValue::Literal(val) => DynamicValue::Literal(*val),
   681	                                    DynamicValue::Ref(r) => {
   682	                                        DynamicValue::Literal(resolve_f32_default(params, r))
   683	                                    }
   684	                                })
   685	                            };
   686	                        DynamicDimension::Dynamic {
   687	                            min: inst_dv(min),
   688	                            max: inst_dv(max),
   689	                        }
   690	                    }
   691	                }
   692	            };
   693	            TemplateFormat::Single {
   694	                width: inst_dim(width),
   695	                height: inst_dim(height),
   696	                media_width: *media_width,
   697	            }
   698	        }
   699	        TemplateFormat::Sheet { .. } => format.clone(),
   700	    }
   701	}
   702	
   703	fn instantiate_layout_defaults(
   704	    layout: &Layout,
   705	    params: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, ParamSpec>,
   706	) -> Layout {
   707	    match layout {
   708	        Layout::Items(items) => Layout::Items(
   709	            items
   710	                .iter()
   711	                .map(|item| instantiate_item_defaults(item, params))
   712	                .collect(),
   713	        ),
   714	    }
   715	}
   716

   718	    item: &LayoutItem,
   719	    params: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, ParamSpec>,

   721	    let inst_placement = |placement: &crate::models::Placement| -> crate::models::Placement {

   731	                };

   740	            max_h: placement.max_h,
   741	            rotate: placement.rotate,
   742	        }
   743	    };

  1159	/// True if any item in the subtree uses an `auto` width or height. Used to forbid auto sizing
  1160	/// anywhere inside a rotated container (#98), where author-horizontal maps to physical-vertical
  1161	/// and the dynamic-width measurement model does not apply.
  1162	fn subtree_uses_auto(items: &[LayoutItem]) -> bool {
  1163	    items.iter().any(|item| match item {
  1164	        LayoutItem::Text { placement, .. }
  1165	        | LayoutItem::Qr { placement, .. }
  1166	        | LayoutItem::Image { placement, .. } => placement
  1167	            .size_or_auto()
  1168	            .is_some_and(|s| s.0[0].is_auto() || s.0[1].is_auto()),
  1169	        LayoutItem::Container {
  1170	            placement, items, ..
  1171	        } => {
  1172	            placement
  1173	                .size_or_auto()
  1174	                .is_some_and(|s| s.0[0].is_auto() || s.0[1].is_auto())
  1175	                || subtree_uses_auto(items)
  1176	        }
  1177	        LayoutItem::Line { .. } => false,
  1178	    })
  1179	}

  1181	fn validate_rotation(rotate: &Option<f32>, is_container: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
  1182	    if let Some(deg) = rotate {
  1183	        // Rotation is a container-only inner transform (#98); reject it on any other item,
  1184	        // regardless of value (even 0).
  1185	        if !is_container {
  1186	            return Err("rotation is only supported on containers".to_string());
  1187	        }
  1188	        if crate::models::Rotation::from_degrees(*deg).is_none() {
  1189	            return Err("rotate must be a multiple of 90 degrees".to_string());
  1190	        }
  1191	    }
  1192	    Ok(())
  1193	}
