   726	/// State threaded through the render pass for dynamic-width auto-length labels.
   727	struct AutoLength<'a> {
   728	    texts: &'a [MeasuredText],
   729	    cursor: &'a Cell<usize>,
   730	}
   731	
   732	/// Whether this frame is on a dynamic-width (auto-length) label. `Dynamic` carries the measured
   733	/// texts, which may legitimately be empty: a label can be sized by lines or containers alone.
   734	/// This is a property of the template format and must never be inferred from `texts.is_empty()`.
   735	enum LengthMode<'a> {
   736	    Fixed,
   737	    Dynamic(AutoLength<'a>),
   738	}
   739	
   740	/// Render-time environment: the variables map and the datetime resolver, passed together through
   741	/// every render call so related configuration travels as a unit.
   742	struct RenderEnv<'a> {
   743	    settings: &'a BTreeMap<String, String>,
   744	    datetime: &'a crate::datetime_fmt::DateTimeResolver<'a>,
   745	}
   746	
   747	/// The anchor of a leaf box item, for `measure`'s clause 1 (a right-anchored item cannot define
   748	/// the width it is anchored to, so it is skipped entirely). `Container` is excluded on purpose: a
   749	/// container's own position can be right-anchored while its *inner frame* is not (its width is
   750	/// still known, since `validate_placement_position` forbids pairing an edge-relative `at.x` with a
   751	/// frame-dependent width on a dynamic-width template), so its children must still be measured.
   752	/// `measure` gives `Container` its own clause-1 branch instead of using this. `Line` has two
   753	/// endpoints and no box, so it is handled separately too.
   754	fn item_anchor(item: &LayoutItem) -> Option<&Position> {
   755	    match item {
   756	        LayoutItem::Text { placement, .. }
   757	        | LayoutItem::Qr { placement, .. }
   758	        | LayoutItem::Image { placement, .. } => Some(&placement.at),
   759	        LayoutItem::Line { .. } | LayoutItem::Container { .. } => None,
   760	    }

    39	THE GITHUB ISSUE THIS CHANGE IMPLEMENTS (#180, verbatim):
    40	---8<---
    41	Title: Horizontal alignment is ignored for auto-width text on auto-length templates
    42	
    43	## Summary
    44	`alignment.horizontal` is silently ignored for auto-width text on a dynamic-width (auto-length)
    45	template. The text always renders flush against `at.x`, even when the label is wider than the text.
    46	
    47	## Reproduce
    48	Serve `catalog/tape/brother/brother_12mm.yaml` (its text item is `size: [auto, 7.9]` with
    49	`alignment: {horizontal: center, vertical: center}`), then POST /api/render/label?format=png with
    50	{"template":"brother_12mm","data":{"message":"Hi"}}.
    51	Expected: "Hi" centered on the 10mm label (`width.min`). Actual: 71x70 px PNG with the glyphs flush
    52	against the 1mm left padding. Raising `width.min` to 40.0 makes it unmistakable: a 283 px label with
    53	"Hi" at the far left.
    54	
    55	## Cause
    56	`brother_12mm` has `format.width: {min, max}`, so it is a dynamic-width label, and the text's
    57	`size[0]` is `auto`, so `Placement::width_is_frame_dependent()` is true and rendering takes the
    58	auto-length branch at src/render/mod.rs:1364. In that branch, for an `Extent::Size` the box width is
    59	the measured fitted width of the text itself (`Extent::Size(_) => m.width`). The
    60	`#align({vertical} + {horizontal})` emitted therefore centers the text inside a box that is exactly
    61	its own width, which is a no-op, and the box is then placed at `dx = at.x`. The fixed-width path
    62	below calls `resolve_size(..., allow_auto_fill: true)`, so its box fills the parent frame and
    63	centering works there. This is the only difference between the two paths.
    64	The enclosing container is not at fault: on a dynamic label an auto-width container already spans the
    65	full remaining frame width, so in the repro there is 8mm of real slack that the text box declines to
    66	occupy.
    67	
    68	## Why it usually hides
    69	On an auto-length label the page width is `content_extent.clamp(min_w, max_w)`, so the label normally
    70	shrinks to the text and there is no slack to center within. It only surfaces when the clamp opens a
    71	gap: content narrower than `width.min`, or wider than `width.max`.
    72	
    73	## Fix shape
    74	On the auto-length `Extent::Size` path, when `alignment.horizontal` is not `Left`, size the box to
    75	the available frame remainder (`frame_width_units - left`, capped by `max_w`) instead of `m.width`.
    76	The measure pass must keep contributing the fitted width, not the frame width, or the label would
    77	never shrink to its content. Only the render-time box changes. Vertical alignment is unaffected.
    78	
    79	## Acceptance
    80	- Short text on a `brother_12mm` clamped to `width.min` renders horizontally centered.
    81	- `horizontal: right` puts the text at the right edge of the same slot.
    82	- Text longer than `width.min` still sizes the label to its content (no regression in the auto-length clamp).
    83	- A render test asserts the emitted box width, not just that it renders.
    84	---8<---
    85

    87	verdict. Judge the plan (proposal + specs + design) only.

   149	     1	# Labeler
   150	     2	
   151	     3	A stateless label-rendering REST service (Rust/axum). It loads YAML label templates from
   152	     4	`{LABELER_CONFIG_DIR}/templates/` and renders a single label to PNG or a batch to PDF/ZIP, by
   153	     5	generating [Typst](https://typst.app/) source on the fly and compiling it in-process via
   154	     6	`typst-as-lib`.
   155	     7	
   156	     8	## Where behavior is specified
   157	     9	
   158	    10	Two places, and the split is deliberate (ADR-0057).
   159	    11	
   160	    12	`docs/SPEC.md` is **frozen** at commit `bc7b1ce` (2026-08-19): the baseline for the API, template
   161	    13	schema, layout model, coordinates, and error contract as of that date. Do not edit it, and do not add
   162	    14	changelog entries to it.
   163	    15	
   164	    16	`openspec/specs/<capability>/spec.md` holds everything added or changed since. It starts empty and
   165	    17	accrues one capability at a time.
   166	    18	
   167	    19	**Precedence.** A frozen `docs/SPEC.md` section stays authoritative until an OpenSpec requirement
   168	    20	explicitly names and supersedes it, and then only for that section. To look a rule up: read
   169	    21	`docs/SPEC.md`, then check `openspec/specs/` for a requirement superseding it.
   170	    22	
   171	    23	**First-touch.** The first change to behavior documented only in the frozen spec writes an `ADDED`
   172	    24	requirement holding the *complete* post-change contract, not the difference, naming the `docs/SPEC.md`
   173	    25	section it supersedes. A `MODIFIED` delta is only valid against a requirement that already exists in
   174	    26	`openspec/specs/`: the tooling resolves `MODIFIED` by locating that requirement, so a `MODIFIED`
   175	    27	against an unmigrated section has nothing to resolve against.
   176	    28	
   177	    29	`docs/adr/` holds append-only Nygard ADRs. Every behavior change adds or supersedes an ADR and adds
   178	    30	its row to `docs/adr/README.md`, in the same change. Supersede rather than edit.
   179	    31	
   180	    32	Also in `docs/`: `WORKFLOW.md` (how changes get made, for humans), `AUTHORING.md` (template model by worked example), `VISION.md`, `DEPLOY.md`.
   181	    33	
   182	    34	## Tracking work
   183	    35	
   184	    36	GitHub issues and milestones are the sole live tracker. No markdown TODOs, no roadmap docs. File with
   185	    37	`gh issue create` and reference from commits (`Fixes #12`). Work you won't do now becomes an issue,
   186	    38	never a TODO in code or docs, and never an unchecked task in `tasks.md`.

   190	    42	exists; its scratch stays under `docs/superpowers/` (gitignored).
   191	    43	
   192	    44	## OpenSpec workflow
   193	    45	
   194	    46	Behavior changes go through OpenSpec (CLI 1.9.0) on this project's own schema,
   195	    47	`openspec/schemas/labeler/`: `proposal → specs → design → review → tasks → apply`. Order matters,
   196	    48	because the review gates implementation and archive rewrites the main specs *after* it:
   197	    49	
   198	    50	1. **Issue**, then a worktree: `git worktree add .worktrees/issue-<N> -b issue-<N>-<slug>`.
   199	    51	2. **`/opsx:propose`** writes `openspec/changes/issue-<N>-<slug>/`. Planning only; it must not touch
   200	    52	   code. Link the issue in `proposal.md`.
   201	    53	3. **Adversarial review of the plan**, before any task is written: the `review` artifact, judging
   202	    54	   `proposal.md` + `specs/` + `design.md`. A second model in read-only mode, else a fresh-context
   203	    55	   subagent. **Never** self-review inside the authoring context. It writes `review.md` ending in a
   204	    56	   `VERDICT:` line. `REVISE` → fix the artifacts and re-run the *full* review in a fresh context;
   205	    57	   `APPROVE_WITH_CHANGES` → apply the listed edits, reviewer re-checks only those, then set
   206	    58	   `CHANGES_APPLIED: yes`. Editing the specs or design afterwards voids the verdict.
   207	    59	
   208	    60	   **This is the only place a human enters the loop, and only on failure.** Two consecutive `REVISE`
   209	    61	   rounds is a hard stop: do not implement, do not keep retrying. Surface `review.md` and the
   210	    62	   artifacts, and wait. On the converging path the loop runs unattended through to the merge.
   211	    63	4. **`/opsx:apply`**, then the adversarial review of the *diff* below. Two different reviews: step 3
   212	    64	   judged the plan, this one judges the code. Do not skip it because tasks are checked.
   213	    65	   The gate is `scripts/review-gate-check.sh`, run by `.githooks/pre-commit` and by CI, so it applies
   214	    66	   to every agent equally: it judges files, not which tool produced them. It refuses a commit touching
   215	    67	   `src/` or `ui/src/` while the change's verdict has not passed, and refuses a review whose `AUTHOR:`
   216	    68	   and `REVIEWER:` match. `.claude/hooks/review-gate.sh` calls the same script at edit time as an
   217	    69	   early signal for Claude Code only. Enable the hooks once per clone with `./scripts/setup-hooks.sh`.
   218	    70	5. **`/opsx:archive`**, always syncing every delta into `openspec/specs/`. Archive is advisory and
   219	    71	   will offer to skip the sync or accept unchecked tasks; both are forbidden here. Out-of-scope tasks
   220	    72	   get cut and filed as issues.
   221	    73	6. **Review the archive diff.** It rewrote `openspec/specs/` after your last review pass.
   222	    74	7. **Verify**, then one commit covering code, ADR, specs, and the archived change, with `Fixes #N`.
   223	    75	
   224	    76	[`docs/WORKFLOW.md`](docs/WORKFLOW.md) describes this loop for a human reader: what it guarantees
   225	    77	and when it stops for them. It carries no commands. Mechanics belong here, not there.
   226	    78	
   227	    79	`tasks.md` is execution state for one accepted issue, never a backlog. That is what keeps the
   228	    80	"issues are the sole tracker" rule intact.
   229	    81	
   230	    82	`openspec/config.yaml` (`context`, `rules.*`, `operations.*.guidance`) is what the `opsx` workflows
   231	    83	inject into each artifact. It restates these rules on purpose, so the workflow stands alone. Change a
   232	    84	process rule here and change it there too.
   233	    85	
   234	    86	`openspec/schemas/labeler/` is a **fork** of the built-in `spec-driven` schema, so it does not inherit
   235	    87	upstream improvements. On a CLI upgrade, diff it against the new built-in and port what matters; the
   236	    88	command is in the schema's header comment. Its `review` artifact is adapted from the `anvil` community
   237	    89	schema by @jikkujoyce, minus the TDD stages.
   238	    90	
   239	    91	The `openspec-*` skills and `opsx` commands under `.claude/`, `.agent/`, `.agents/`, `.opencode/` are
   240	    92	**generated** (43 files; the 24 `SKILL.md` manifests record `generatedBy: 1.9.0`). Never hand-edit

/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba src/render/helpers.rs | sed -n '620,760p'" in /home/pfa/projects/labeler/.worktrees/issue-180
 succeeded in 0ms:
   620	fn ascent_overflow_em(face: &ttf_parser::Face) -> f32 {
   621	    let upem = f32::from(face.units_per_em());
   622	    let cap = face
   623	        .capital_height()
   624	        .filter(|v| *v > 0)
   625	        .map(f32::from)
   626	        .unwrap_or_else(|| typo_ascender(face));
   627	    ((typo_ascender(face) - cap) / upem).max(0.0)
   628	}
   629	
   630	/// Ink below the baseline, as a fraction of the em: where descenders live.
   631	fn descent_overflow_em(face: &ttf_parser::Face) -> f32 {
   632	    (-typo_descender(face) / f32::from(face.units_per_em())).max(0.0)
   633	}
   634	
   635	/// The inset the renderer emits at the aligned edge, so ink stays inside the clipped slot (#124).
   636	fn pad_em(face: &ttf_parser::Face, vertical: VerticalAlign) -> f32 {
   637	    match vertical {
   638	        VerticalAlign::Top => ascent_overflow_em(face),
   639	        VerticalAlign::Bottom => descent_overflow_em(face),
   640	        VerticalAlign::Center => 0.0,
   641	    }
   642	}
   643	
   644	/// The pad in points, for callers outside this module: `render/mod.rs` has no `Face` and `instance`
   645	/// stays private.
   646	pub(super) fn pad_pt(weight: u16, size: f32, vertical: VerticalAlign) -> Result<f32, AppError> {
   647	    // Short-circuit before touching the font. Center pads nothing, and loading the measurement face
   648	    // here would give a centered fixed-size render a new way to fail — it would start depending on
   649	    // InterVariable being present and carrying the wght/opsz axes to emit source it does not use.
   650	    if matches!(vertical, VerticalAlign::Center) {
   651	        return Ok(0.0);
   652	    }
   653	    let face = instance(weight, size)?;
   654	    Ok(pad_em(&face, vertical) * size)
   655	}
   656	
   657	/// What the *fitter* holds back so the edge the text is not aligned to cannot clip either: a
   658	/// top-aligned block pushed down by its top pad still has descenders near the slot floor. Both
   659	/// overflows, so it is neither `pad_em` nor the 1.21em band — the cap-height line covers the rest.
   660	fn overflow_em(face: &ttf_parser::Face, vertical: VerticalAlign) -> f32 {
   661	    match vertical {
   662	        VerticalAlign::Top | VerticalAlign::Bottom => {
   663	            ascent_overflow_em(face) + descent_overflow_em(face)
   664	        }
   665	        VerticalAlign::Center => 0.0,
   666	    }
   667	}
   668	
   669	/// Typst's default paragraph leading, 0.65em (`model/par.rs`). Sits *between* lines only.
   670	fn leading(size: f32) -> f32 {
   671	    size * 0.65
   672	}
   673	
   674	/// Height of an `n`-line block as Typst stacks it: leading between lines, not after the last one
   675	/// (typst-layout `collect.rs` pushes it only when `i > 0`). A fused per-line constant — which is what
   676	/// a "line height" is — overshoots by one leading per block and shrinks text that would have fit.
   677	fn block_height(face: &ttf_parser::Face, size: f32, lines: usize, vertical: VerticalAlign) -> f32 {
   678	    let n = lines.max(1) as f32;
   679	    // The overflow is read off *this* face, already instanced at the candidate size: the metrics move
   680	    // with the opsz axis, so a ratio captured earlier would belong to a different instance.
   681	    n * cap_height(face, size) + (n - 1.0) * leading(size) + overflow_em(face, vertical) * size
   682	}
   683

   685	pub(crate) fn block_height_for_test(weight: u16, size: f32, lines: usize) -> f32 {
   686	    let face = instance(weight, size).expect("face");
   687	    block_height(&face, size, lines, VerticalAlign::Center)
   688	}

   696	#[cfg(test)]
   697	pub(crate) fn units_to_pt_for_test(value: f32, unit: &str) -> f32 {
   698	    units_to_pt(value, unit)
   699	}
   700	
   701	fn units_to_pt(value: f32, unit: &str) -> f32 {
   702	    match unit {
   703	        "in" => value * 72.0,
   704	        "mm" => value * 72.0 / 25.4,
   705	        _ => value,
   706	    }
   707	}
   708	
   709	fn pt_to_units(value_pt: f32, unit: &str) -> f32 {
   710	    match unit {
   711	        "in" => value_pt / 72.0,
   712	        "mm" => value_pt * 25.4 / 72.0,
   713	        _ => value_pt,
   714	    }
   715	}
   716	
   717	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
   718	pub(super) struct MeasuredText {
   719	    pub font: f32,
   720	    pub lines: Vec<String>,
   721	    pub width: f32,
   722	}
   723	
   724	/// Auto-length fit: choose the font, return the (possibly wrapped and ellipsized) lines and the
   725	/// natural width (clamped to the budget). Width is in template units.
   726	///
   727	/// When `multiline` is `false`, only the first input line is considered; the result is always
   728	/// one line (ellipsized on overflow). When `multiline` is `true`, the text is word-wrapped to the
   729	/// budget width, the largest font that fits the height is chosen, and overflow lines are ellipsized.
   730	pub(super) fn fit_text_auto_length(
   731	    raw_text: &str,
   732	    font_size: &FontSize,
   733	    multiline: bool,
   734	    weight: u16,
   735	    vertical: VerticalAlign,
   736	    fit: FitBox,
   737	) -> Result<MeasuredText, AppError> {
   738	    let (budget_w_units, unit) = (fit.width_units, fit.unit);
   739	    let budget_pt = units_to_pt(budget_w_units, unit);
   740	    let height_pt = units_to_pt(fit.height_units, unit);
   741	
   742	    if !multiline {
   743	        let line = to_nonbreaking(raw_text.lines().next().unwrap_or(""));
   744	        let size = match font_size {
   745	            FontSize::Fixed(s) => *s,
   746	            FontSize::Range { min, max } => {
   747	                largest_fitting_font(&line, false, weight, vertical, *min, *max, fit)
   748	            }
   749	        };
   750	        let face = instance(weight, size)?;
   751	        let face = &face;
   752	        if text_fits(face, &line, false, size, budget_pt, height_pt, vertical) {
   753	            let w = pt_to_units(text_width(face, &line, size), unit).min(budget_w_units);
   754	            return Ok(MeasuredText {
   755	                font: size,
   756	                lines: vec![line],
   757	                width: w,
   758	            });
   759	        }
   760	        let trimmed = trim_single_line(face, &line, size, budget_pt);

exec
/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba src/render/helpers.rs | sed -n '760,850p'" in /home/pfa/projects/labeler/.worktrees/issue-180
 succeeded in 0ms:
   760	        let trimmed = trim_single_line(face, &line, size, budget_pt);
   761	        return Ok(MeasuredText {
   762	            font: size,
   763	            lines: vec![trimmed],
   764	            width: budget_w_units,
   765	        });
   766	    }
   767	
   768	    // Multiline: pick the font (shrink to fit the height with wrapping), then produce the final lines.
   769	    let size = match font_size {
   770	        FontSize::Fixed(s) => *s,
   771	        FontSize::Range { min, max } => {
   772	            largest_fitting_font(raw_text, true, weight, vertical, *min, *max, fit)
   773	        }
   774	    };
   775	    let face = instance(weight, size)?;
   776	    let (lines, max_w_pt) = wrap_lines_fit(&face, raw_text, size, budget_pt, height_pt, vertical);
   777	    let width = pt_to_units(max_w_pt, unit).min(budget_w_units);
   778	    Ok(MeasuredText {
   779	        font: size,
   780	        lines,
   781	        width,
   782	    })
   783	}
   784	
   785	/// Wrap `text` to `width_pt`, keep only the lines that fit `height_pt` (ellipsizing the last on
   786	/// overflow), and NBSP-treat each kept line so the renderer cannot re-break it. Wrapping happens on
   787	/// real spaces first (NBSP is not whitespace, so it must be applied after wrapping). Returns the
   788	/// NBSP-treated lines plus the longest-line width in points, measured on the raw (pre-NBSP) lines so
   789	/// the width never depends on NBSP and space sharing an advance in the measurement font.
   790	fn wrap_lines_fit(
   791	    face: &ttf_parser::Face,
   792	    text: &str,
   793	    size: f32,
   794	    width_pt: f32,
   795	    height_pt: f32,
   796	    vertical: VerticalAlign,
   797	) -> (Vec<String>, f32) {
   798	    const ELLIPSIS: &str = "...";
   799	    // Inverse of block_height, as in trim_multiline, minus the edge reservation.
   800	    let max_lines = ((height_pt - overflow_em(face, vertical) * size + leading(size))
   801	        / (cap_height(face, size) + leading(size)))
   802	    .floor()
   803	    .max(1.0) as usize;
   804	    let mut lines = wrap_text(face, text, size, width_pt);
   805	    if lines.len() > max_lines {
   806	        lines.truncate(max_lines);
   807	        let last = lines.last_mut().unwrap();
   808	        let ellipsis_width = text_width(face, ELLIPSIS, size);
   809	        if ellipsis_width > width_pt {
   810	            *last = ELLIPSIS.to_string();
   811	        } else {
   812	            while !last.is_empty()
   813	                && text_width(face, &format!("{last}{ELLIPSIS}"), size) > width_pt
   814	            {
   815	                last.pop();
   816	            }
   817	            *last = format!("{last}{ELLIPSIS}");
   818	        }
   819	    }
   820	    let max_w_pt = lines
   821	        .iter()
   822	        .map(|l| text_width(face, l, size))
   823	        .fold(0.0_f32, f32::max);
   824	    let lines = lines.into_iter().map(|l| to_nonbreaking(&l)).collect();
   825	    (lines, max_w_pt)
   826	}
   827	
   828	pub(super) fn typst_alignment(alignment: &Alignment) -> String {
   829	    let horizontal = match alignment.horizontal {
   830	        HorizontalAlign::Left => "left",
   831	        HorizontalAlign::Center => "center",
   832	        HorizontalAlign::Right => "right",
   833	    };
   834	    let vertical = match alignment.vertical {
   835	        VerticalAlign::Top => "top",
   836	        VerticalAlign::Center => "horizon",
   837	        VerticalAlign::Bottom => "bottom",
   838	    };
   839	    format!("{vertical} + {horizontal}")
   840	}

  2904	    /// The cap must be inert when no bound is set. One assertion per site the branch capped, so a
  2905	    /// leak names the site. These pass before and after this branch; they exist to stay green.
  2906	    #[test]
  2907	    fn no_max_w_means_no_cap_anywhere() {
  2908	        // Text: an uncapped auto-width text measures its natural width against the full budget.
  2909	        let long = "a string long enough to have a natural width worth measuring";
  2910	        let (text_extent, _) = measured_extent_of(
  2911	            LayoutItem::Text {
  2912	                value: long.to_string(),
  2913	                placement: Placement {
  2914	                    at: Position([0.0, 0.0]),
  2915	                    extent: crate::models::Extent::Size(Size([
  2916	                        SizeValue::Auto(crate::models::AutoSize::Auto),
  2917	                        SizeValue::fixed(8.0),
  2918	                    ])),
  2919	                    max_w: None,
  2920	                    max_h: None,
  2921	                    rotate: None,
  2922	                },
  2923	                font_size: FontSize::Fixed(10.0),
  2924	                font_weight: None,
  2925	                multiline: false,
  2926	                alignment: crate::models::Alignment::default(),
  2927	                when: None,
  2928	            },
  2929	            200.0,
  2930	        );
  2931	        assert!(text_extent > 0.0 && text_extent < 200.0);
  2932	
  2933	        // Qr: an uncapped auto-width qr still fills the remaining budget.
  2934	        let (qr_extent, _) = measured_extent_of(
  2935	            LayoutItem::Qr {
  2936	                value: "abc".to_string(),
  2937	                placement: Placement {
  2938	                    at: Position([10.0, 0.0]),
  2939	                    extent: crate::models::Extent::Size(Size([
  2940	                        SizeValue::Auto(crate::models::AutoSize::Auto),
  2941	                        SizeValue::fixed(20.0),
  2942	                    ])),
  2943	                    max_w: None,
  2944	                    max_h: None,
  2945	                    rotate: None,
  2946	                },
  2947	                params: None,
  2948	                when: None,
  2949	            },
  2950	            100.0,
  2951	        );
  2952	        assert_eq!(qr_extent, 100.0, "no bound means fill the remaining budget");
  2953	
  2954	        // Container, measurement: the child must be something whose measured width depends on
  2955	        // the inner budget, or the assertion proves nothing. An empty container contributes `at_x`
  2956	        // whatever the budget was, including a budget wrongly capped to zero.
  2957	        let child = LayoutItem::Text {
  2958	            value: long.to_string(),
  2959	            placement: Placement {
  2960	                at: Position([0.0, 0.0]),
  2961	                extent: crate::models::Extent::Size(Size([
  2962	                    SizeValue::Auto(crate::models::AutoSize::Auto),
  2963	                    SizeValue::fixed(8.0),
  2964	                ])),
  2965	                max_w: None,
  2966	                max_h: None,
  2967	                rotate: None,
  2968	            },
  2969	            font_size: FontSize::Fixed(10.0),
  2970	            font_weight: None,
  2971	            multiline: false,
  2972	            alignment: crate::models::Alignment::default(),
  2973	            when: None,
  2974	        };
  2975	        let (c_extent, _) = measured_extent_of(capped_container(10.0, None, vec![child]), 200.0);
  2976	        assert!(
  2977	            (c_extent - (10.0 + text_extent)).abs() < 0.5,
  2978	            "an uncapped container is sized by its child ({text_extent}mm at x=10), got {c_extent}"
  2979	        );
  2980

  2982	        let source = dynamic_ctx_source(100.0, capped_container(10.0, None, vec![]));
  2983	        assert!(
  2984	            source.contains("width: 90mm"),
  2985	            "an uncapped container fills the frame remainder: {source}"
  2986	        );
  2987	    }

  2989	    /// #152. `brother_24mm_weights.yaml` sets `max_w: 117` at `at.x: 1.5` on a `width.max: 120`
  2990	    /// tape, so the budget goes from 118.5 to 117 — a cap that binds by only 1.5mm. With the short
  2991	    /// placeholder data the suite uses, the render must be unchanged: this pins that a cap this
  2992	    /// close to the natural remainder does not perturb a real catalog/fixture template.
  2993	    #[test]
  2994	    fn brother_24mm_weights_render_is_unchanged_by_the_cap() {
  2995	        let registry = crate::templates::load_all_for_tests().0;
  2996	        let capped = registry.get("brother_24mm_weights").expect("template");
  2997	        let TemplateFormat::Single {
  2998	            width:
  2999	                DynamicDimension::Dynamic {
  3000	                    max: Some(DynamicValue::Literal(max_w)),
  3001	                    ..
  3002	                },
  3003	            ..
  3004	        } = &capped.format
  3005	        else {
  3006	            panic!("expected a dynamic-width single format");
  3007	        };
  3008	        assert_eq!(*max_w, 120.0, "budget math below assumes width.max: 120");
  3009	        let data = placeholder_data(capped);
  3010	        let capped_png = render_thumbnail_png(capped, &data, None, &no_settings(), &no_datetime())
  3011	            .expect("render capped");
  3012	
  3013	        // Same template, `max_w` stripped from both text items: the fallback remainder is
  3014	        // `width.max - at.x` = 118.5mm, so 117mm binds by only 1.5mm. With the short placeholder
  3015	        // text this suite uses, neither budget is the constraint that decides the fitted font
  3016	        // size or width, so the two renders must be pixel-identical.
  3017	        let mut uncapped = capped.clone();
  3018	        let Layout::Items(items) = &mut uncapped.layout;
  3019	        for item in items {
  3020	            if let LayoutItem::Text { placement, .. } = item {
  3021	                placement.max_w = None;
  3022	            }
  3023	        }
  3024	        let uncapped_png =
  3025	            render_thumbnail_png(&uncapped, &data, None, &no_settings(), &no_datetime())
  3026	                .expect("render uncapped");
  3027	
  3028	        assert_eq!(
  3029	            capped_png, uncapped_png,
  3030	            "a 117mm cap on a 118.5mm remainder must not change the render with short placeholder text"
  3031	        );
  3032	    }

codex
The formula itself looks defensible: measurement budgets cap `m.width`, final frames are rebased correctly for containers, and `check_box_bounds` should only see pre-existing overflows. The remaining likely findings are documentation precision rather than a broken implementation strategy.

codex
## Findings

### Critical (blocking)

None.

### Moderate

1. The design falsely says right-anchored auto text items are “skipped by both passes.” Measurement skips right-anchored leaf items at `src/render/mod.rs:927-934`, and the auto-length replay branch also skips them via `!placement.at.x().is_sign_negative()` at `src/render/mod.rs:1364-1365`, but the render pass does not skip rendering them: it falls through to the fixed-size path at `src/render/mod.rs:1449-1495`. Top-level dynamic right-anchored auto width is invalid per `docs/SPEC.md:599-601` and validation at `src/templates.rs:1141-1144`; allowed nested/fixed-container cases still render. This makes `openspec/changes/issue-180-auto-length-text-alignment/design.md:47-48` inaccurate and risks confusing the task author.

2. The delta spec’s broad opening sentence covers all dynamic-width `single` auto-width text, but it does not explicitly exclude or defer right-anchored `at.x` cases even though the design declares them out of scope. The frozen coordinate rule still says a right-anchored `at.x` cannot be combined with auto/frame-dependent width on a dynamic-width template at `docs/SPEC.md:599-601`, but the new requirement at `openspec/changes/issue-180-auto-length-text-alignment/specs/auto-length-layout/spec.md:11-34` reads as if it applies universally. Add a sentence tying this requirement to non-edge-relative `at.x` or explicitly saying §6 remains authoritative for right-anchored `at.x`.

3. `design.md` claims “The task list carries an explicit render-and-look step” at `openspec/changes/issue-180-auto-length-text-alignment/design.md:144-146`, but `tasks.md` is intentionally absent at this stage and the change directory currently contains no task artifact. The repo requires visual render inspection for rendering/template changes at `AGENTS.md:160-167`, and task gates are required later by `openspec/config.yaml:77-85`. Reword this as a requirement for the future task list, not as a current fact.

### Suggestions

Verified citation accuracy: the core proposal citations are correct. `Extent::Size(_) => m.width` is at `src/render/mod.rs:1401`; `#align(...)` is emitted at `src/render/mod.rs:1426`; the fixed path starts resolving size at `src/render/mod.rs:1449` with `allow_auto_fill: true` at `src/render/mod.rs:1454`; the clamp is at `src/render/mod.rs:341-342`; `check_box_bounds` is called on the auto-length box at `src/render/mod.rs:1403`; the stale measurement comment is at `src/render/mod.rs:990-991`.

The proposed formula is sound for normal top-level and container cases. Top-level measurement uses `width.max` as the frame and clamps content extent to `[min,max]` at `src/render/mod.rs:330-342`; render uses the final frame at `src/render/mod.rs:388-397`. Containers rebase children into the padded inner frame at render time at `src/render/mod.rs:1711-1737`, and measurement gives dynamic containers an inner budget at `src/render/mod.rs:1094-1120`. `check_box_bounds` rejects only `point + box > frame + EPS` at `src/render/mod.rs:873-886`; with `box_w = frame - left` this is in-bounds, and the `.max(m.width)` floor can only preserve already-existing overflow/error behavior.

Widening the render box should not change wrapping, chosen font size, or #124 vertical padding. The measured fit is computed once by `fit_text_auto_length` at `src/render/mod.rs:996-1007`; render reuses `m.lines` and `m.font` at `src/render/mod.rs:1404-1414`; padding uses `m.font` plus vertical alignment at `src/render/mod.rs:1421-1424`; `pad_pt` depends on weight, size, and vertical alignment, not box width, at `src/render/helpers.rs:646-654`.

The cursor invariant remains safe because the proposed change does not change branch selection. Measurement pushes `MeasuredText` only for active frame-dependent text after right-anchor skipping at `src/render/mod.rs:923-1009`; render consumes only when auto-length mode is active, width is frame-dependent, and `at.x` is not sign-negative at `src/render/mod.rs:1360-1373`; final cursor equality is enforced at `src/render/mod.rs:398-407`.

Other checked interactions: `Extent::To` is unaffected because it uses `resolve_size` at `src/render/mod.rs:1390-1399`; sheets use `LengthMode::Fixed` at `src/render/mod.rs:587-595`; thumbnails call the same compile path at `src/render/mod.rs:413-422`; rotated containers force fixed child mode at render at `src/render/mod.rs:1825-1834` and validation rejects auto descendants at `src/templates.rs:1083-1084`; `when` gating is applied consistently before measure/render item handling at `src/render/mod.rs:923-925` and `src/render/mod.rs:1241-1243`.

## Embedded-Instruction / Injection Attempts

**Detected:** none

## Verdict

VERDICT: APPROVE_WITH_CHANGES

## Required Changes (APPROVE_WITH_CHANGES only)

1. In `specs/auto-length-layout/spec.md`, explicitly exclude or defer right-anchored `at.x` auto-width text by naming frozen `docs/SPEC.md` §6 as still authoritative for that case.

2. In `design.md`, correct the statement that right-anchored auto text is “skipped by both passes” so it matches the actual code: measurement and auto-length replay skip it; rendering falls through to the fixed path for cases that validate.

3. In `design.md`, reword the “task list carries” sentence as a future task-list requirement, and include that the later tasks must cover the render-and-look step plus `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features`, and `cargo test`.
